```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "For the Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ cards, we prove that the worst-start total-variation distance after $`32800d`$ physical shuffles tends to zero as $`d\\to\\infty`$. Combining our fixed-list estimate with the companion Fourier transfer improves this bound to $`512d`$ shuffles. These results follow from bounds on partially observed permutation laws in random coordinate frames.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For the Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ cards, we prove that the worst-start total-variation distance after $`32800d`$ physical shuffles tends to zero as $`d\to\infty`$. Combining our fixed-list estimate with the companion Fourier transfer improves this bound to $`512d`$ shuffles. These results follow from bounds on partially observed permutation laws in random coordinate frames.

- **claim-standalone-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For $n=2^d$, $$\\|q_d^{*(32800d)}-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0 \qquad(d\longrightarrow\infty).$$ The convergence is uniform over every deterministic initial ordering of the deck.
  - Explanation: The physical shuffle approaches uniformity from every deterministic starting deck at the stated linear horizon.
- **claim-support-obstruction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lower)): For every integer $t\ge0$, $$\label{eq:exact-support-lower} \\|q_d^{*t}-U\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\ge 1-\frac{2^{tn/2}}{n!}.$$ Consequently, $$\label{eq:sharp-support-time} t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\ge \left\lceil\frac2n\log_2\left(\frac{3n!}{4}\right)\right\rceil =2d-O(1).$$ For $0\le t\le d$, $$\\|q_d^{*t}-U\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\ge1-(e/\sqrt n)^n.$$ In particular, the distance tends to one uniformly for integer $t\le d$, and $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\ge d$ for all sufficiently large $d$.
  - Explanation: Counting available switch outcomes obstructs early full-deck mixing and yields the sharper asymptotic lower bound.
- **claim-single-card-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-one-card-lower)): For a fixed initial card and an integer $0\le t<d$, its location law has support at most $2^t$. Consequently the full permutation law has total-variation distance at least $1-2^t/n$ from uniform, and $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\ge d$. After $d$ steps every individual card has a uniform location.
  - Explanation: A card has restricted support before a complete coordinate sweep, although its location becomes uniform after that sweep.
- **claim-fixed-schedule-frame** (construction, [source](#stmt-shear-lem-general-frame)): Let $T\ge d$, and let $\boldsymbol\iota=(\iota_1,\ldots,\iota_d)$ be a permutation of $1,\ldots,d$. Write $i(t)=\iota_{1+((t-1)\bmod d)}$, and let $X^{\boldsymbol\iota}_0=\mathrm{id}$ and $X^{\boldsymbol\iota}_t=Q^{\boldsymbol\iota}_t X^{\boldsymbol\iota}_{t-1}$, where the increments are independent and uniform in $C_{e_{i(t)}}$.* *Fix directions $v_1,\ldots,v_T$ satisfying $$\label{eq:frames:bases} (v_s,\ldots,v_{s+d-1})\text{ is a basis of }V \qquad(1\le s\le T-d+1).$$ Let $Z_0=\mathrm{id}$, and let $Z_t$ evolve by independent uniform switches in these directions. There are linear bijections $L_0,\ldots,L_T$, determined by the fixed axis order and direction schedule, such that $$\label{shear:eq:general-frame} (Z_t)_{t=0}^T\overset{\mathrm{law}}= \bigl(L_tX^{\boldsymbol\iota}_tL_0^{-1}\bigr)_{t=0}^T, \qquad L_0e_{\iota_j}=v_j\quad(1\le j\le d).$$ In particular, if $\boldsymbol\iota=(1,\ldots,d)$ and $v_j=e_j$ for $1\le j\le d$, then $L_0=I$ and $$\label{eq:frames:endpoint} Z_T\overset{\mathrm{law}}=L_TX_T=L_TR^{-T}Y_T.$$
  - Explanation: Deterministic linear frames identify complete path laws after the direction schedule is fixed; the initial frame also determines the input relabeling.
- **claim-endpoint-input-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-frames-marginals)): Under Lemma <a href="#lem:frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:frames">[lem:frames]</a>, let $0\le q\le n$ and let $\pi_q$ be uniform on ordered distinct $q$-tuples of positions. For every fixed ordered list $C$ of $q$ distinct labels, $$\label{eq:frames:tuple-input-map} \\|\mathcal L(Z_T(C))-\pi_q\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} = \\|\mathcal L(X^{\boldsymbol\iota}_T(L_0^{-1}C))-\pi_q\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.$$ Thus the same-list distances agree when $L_0=I$. Their averages also agree when $C$ is sampled uniformly over ordered distinct lists, independently of the switch process: $$\label{eq:frames:averaged-input-map} \mathbb E_C\\|\mathcal L(Z_T(C)\mid C)-\pi_q\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} = \mathbb E_C\\|\mathcal L(X^{\boldsymbol\iota}_T(C)\mid C)-\pi_q\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.$$
  - Explanation: Output bijections preserve tuple distance, while a general initial frame changes the fixed input list. Uniform input averaging removes that change.
- **claim-online-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-shear-lem-energy)): In the preceding setup, for $0\le t<T$ and $1\le j\le q$, $$\label{eq:marginal-exact-decrement} \Delta_{t+1}^j=\Delta_t^j-\frac12 \sum_{\substack{\{x,y\}\subseteq D_t^j\\y=x+v_{t+1}}} \bigl(p_t^j(x)-p_t^j(y)\bigr)^2 \quad\text{almost surely},$$ where each unordered matching edge is counted once. If $K_t$ is uniform on the complement of an $\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable hyperplane $J_t$, then $$\label{eq:marginal-cross-energy} \mathbb E[\Delta_t^j-\Delta_{t+1}^j\mid\mathcal F_t^j] \ge \frac{\min(\|D_t^j\cap J_t\|,\|D_t^j\setminus J_t\|)}{n}\Delta_t^j.$$ More generally, suppose $K_t$ is uniform outside an $\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable proper subspace $W_t$, and let $J_t$ be an $\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable hyperplane containing $W_t$. Then the same right side with denominator $2n$ is a valid lower bound.
  - Explanation: Matching averages give an exact energy decrement. Fresh directions turn cross-hyperplane balance into conditional contraction.
- **claim-terminal-tuple-comparison** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-shear-lem-tuple)): In the preceding setup let $\Lambda$ be terminal data that determine the complete direction schedule, and suppose $\Xi$ is independent of $(\Lambda,(Z_t)_{t=0}^T)$. For every $1\le j\le q$ and every $x\in V$, assume that its online terminal vector is also the conditional hidden law after these data and the complete predecessor paths are known: $$\label{eq:marginal-terminal-identification} \Pr\{Z_T(c_j)=x\mid \Lambda,\Xi,\, Z_s(c_i):0\le s\le T,\ i<j\} =p_T^j(x)\quad\text{almost surely}.$$ Then $$\label{shear:eq:tuple-bound} \mathbb E_{\Lambda,\Xi} \\|\mathcal L(Z_T(C)\mid\Lambda,\Xi)-\pi_q\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le\frac12\sum_{j=1}^q \sqrt{(n-j+1)\mathbb E\Delta_T^j}.$$ All expectations are under the actual joint law of the data, list, and process. If $\mathbb E\Delta_T^j\le a_T$ for every $j$, the right side is at most $q\sqrt n\,\sqrt{a_T}/2$. For $q=0$ both sides are zero.
  - Explanation: Terminal posterior identification permits sequential comparison of the tuple with a uniform injection using expected hidden-card energies.
- **claim-selected-partition** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-marginals)): There is a deterministic labeled partition $\mathcal M=(M_1,\ldots,M_k)$ into blocks of size $m$ for which $$\label{eq:marginal-partition} \sum_{a=1}^k\delta_a(\mathcal M) \le k\varepsilon_n=o(1)\qquad(d\longrightarrow\infty).$$
  - Explanation: Averaging an independent partition proves that some deterministic partition has vanishing summed complementary marginal error.
- **claim-common-trimming** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-trim)): Let $H_1,\ldots,H_k$ be subgroups of a finite group $G$, and let $\mu$ be a probability measure on $G$. Put $$\delta_a=\\|\mu_{H_a}-(U_G)_{H_a}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}, \qquad \delta=\sum_{a=1}^k\delta_a.$$ If $\delta<1/2$, there is a set $E\subseteq G$, of removed mass $\varepsilon=\mu(G\setminus E)\le 2\delta$, such that the conditional probability measure $\bar\mu=\mu(\,\cdot\mid E)$ satisfies $$\label{eq:lift:trim} \\|\bar\mu-\mu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\varepsilon, \qquad \bar\mu(gH_a) \le \frac{2}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{\|H_a\|}{\|G\|} \quad(g\in G,\ 1\le a\le k).$$ In particular, if $\delta\le 1/4$, the last density bound is at most $4\|H_a\|/\|G\|$.
  - Explanation: Deleting the union of heavy cosets produces a common nearby probability law with simultaneous coset caps.
- **claim-orbit-degree-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-lift-orbit-sum)): Let a finite set $X$ be partitioned into $k\ge1$ nonempty sets $M_1,\ldots,M_k$, and put $G=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(X)$. Let $H_a$ be the subgroup permuting $M_a$ and fixing $X\setminus M_a$ pointwise. Suppose that a probability measure $\nu$ on $G$ satisfies, for some $B\ge 1$, $$\label{eq:lift:coset-cap} \nu(gH_a)\le B\frac{\|H_a\|}{\|G\|} \qquad(g\in G,\ 1\le a\le k).$$ Then every irreducible complex unitary representation $\rho$ of $G$, of dimension $D$, satisfies $$\label{eq:lift:orbit-sum} \\|\widehat\nu(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \le \frac{B}{D}\sum_{a=1}^k \sum_{\substack{\pi\in\widehat H_a\\\dim\pi\le D^{1/k}}} (\dim\pi)^2.$$ The block sizes need not be equal.
  - Explanation: Disjoint block restrictions assign each product type to a small factor. A single-vector orbit bound controls Fourier contraction without limiting multiplicities.
- **claim-inverse-sixteenth-summability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-degrees)): There is an absolute finite constant $$C_0:=\sup_{s\ge 1}\sum_{\lambda\vdash s}(f^\lambda)^{-16}<\infty. \label{eq:degrees:uniform}$$ Moreover, $$\sum_{\substack{\lambda\vdash s\\ \lambda\notin\{(s),(1^s)\}}} (f^\lambda)^{-16}\longrightarrow 0 \qquad\text{as }s\longrightarrow\infty. \label{eq:degrees:vanishing}$$ The excluded partitions are interpreted as a set, so when $s=1$ only the single partition $(1)$ is excluded.
  - Explanation: Tableau counting bounds the complete inverse-degree sum uniformly and makes the nonlinear contribution vanish with group size.
- **claim-coset-fourier-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-lift)): Let a finite set $X$ be partitioned into $k>18$ nonempty sets $M_1,\ldots,M_k$, and put $G=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(X)$. Let $H_a$ be the subgroup that permutes $M_a$ and fixes $X\setminus M_a$ pointwise. Suppose that a probability measure $\nu$ on $G$ satisfies, for some $B\ge1$, $$\nu(gH_a)\le B\frac{\|H_a\|}{\|G\|} \qquad(g\in G,\ 1\le a\le k).$$ Then every irreducible complex unitary representation $\rho$ of $G$, of dimension $D$, satisfies $$\label{eq:lift:general-fourier} \\|\widehat\nu(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le\sqrt{kBC_0}\,D^{-(1-18/k)/2}.$$ Here $C_0$ is the absolute constant of Lemma <a href="#lem:degrees" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:degrees">12</a>. The block sizes need not be equal.
  - Explanation: Combining the orbit estimate with inverse-degree summability gives dimension decay uniformly over unequal block sizes.
- **claim-full-group-plancherel** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-plancherel)): For any probability measure $\nu$ on $G$, $$\label{eq:plancherel-tv} 4\\|\nu-U\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}^2 \le\sum_{\rho\ne\mathrm{triv}}D_\rho \\|\widehat\nu(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2,$$ where the sum contains one representative of each nontrivial irreducible equivalence class.
  - Explanation: A dimension-weighted sum of unnormalized Fourier matrix norms bounds full-group total variation.
- **claim-fair-parity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fair-sign)): For every $d\ge1$ and every integer $t\ge1$, $\widehat{q_d^{*t}}(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}})=0$. If a probability measure $\nu$ is $\varepsilon$ away from $q_d^{*t}$ in total variation, then $\|\widehat\nu(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}})\|\le2\varepsilon$. If instead $0\le\xi\le q_d^{*t}$ has mass $m$, then $\|\widehat\xi(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}})\|\le1-m$.
  - Explanation: An independent terminal switch cancels parity exactly; nearby probabilities and retained masses have controlled parity errors.
- **claim-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-concentration)): Let $X_1,\ldots,X_r$ be independent random variables in $[0,1]$ and put $S=\sum_iX_i$. For $a\ge0$ and $r\ge1$, $$\Pr(S-\mathbb ES\ge a),\quad\Pr(S-\mathbb ES\le-a)\le e^{-2a^2/r}.$$ More generally, if $(M_j)_{j=0}^r$ is a real martingale whose increments have absolute value at most $c_j$, either tail at distance $a$ is at most $\exp[-a^2/(2\sum_jc_j^2)]$ when the denominator is positive. If all $c_j$ vanish, the martingale is constant. In particular, if $X$ counts the points of a uniform $m$-subset in a fixed half of a finite set of even size, then $$\Pr(X<m/4)\le e^{-m/32}\qquad(m\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: Exponential moments give independent-variable and martingale tails, including a uniform-subset half-count estimate.
- **claim-transverse-fixed-list-balance** (bound, [source](#stmt-shear-lem-fixed-balance)): Fix any $\ell<N$ observed labels, and put $m=N-\ell$. For $s>d$, let $D_{s-1}$ be their available positions just before step $s$. Then $$\label{shear:eq:fixed-balance} \Pr\{\min(\|D_{s-1}\cap W_s\|,\|D_{s-1}\setminus W_s\|)<m/4\} \le2e^{-m/8}.$$ The probability averages the observed paths; it is not conditional on them.
  - Explanation: A previous transverse switch balances available positions for a fixed list; the probability averages observed paths.
- **claim-uniform-fixed-list-marginals** (bound, [source](#stmt-shear-prop-fixed-marginal)): Let $L\ge2$ be a fixed integer dividing $N$, and let $0\le q\le N-N/L$. For every ordered list $C$ of $q$ distinct labels, every periodic ordering $\boldsymbol\iota$ of the standard axes, and every integer $T\ge d$, $$\label{shear:eq:general-fixed-marginal} \\|\mathcal L(X^{\boldsymbol\iota}_T(C))-\pi_q\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le\frac{N^{3/2}}2 \left(e^{-(T-d)/(4L)}+2e^{-N/(8L)}\right)^{1/2}.$$ The same bound holds for $(X^{\boldsymbol\iota}_T)^{-1}(C)$. For the physical shuffle and its inverse, the corresponding position laws satisfy the same bound.* *The following choices used below give distance at most $N^{3/2}\sqrt{a_T}/2$: $$\begin{aligned} L=8,\quad T=256d:&\quad a_T=(31/32)^{255d}+64e^{-N/64}, \label{shear:eq:256-marginal}\\ L=8,\quad T=257d:&\quad a_T=e^{-8d}+2e^{-N/64}, \label{shear:eq:257-marginal}\\ L=32,\quad T=2049d:&\quad a_T=e^{-16d}+256e^{-N/256}. \label{shear:eq:2049-marginal} \end{aligned}$$ Each of these three substituted bounds tends to zero as $d\to\infty$, uniformly in the list and axis order. The last energy bound is $o(N^{-4})$.
  - Explanation: The balance recurrence and terminal frame comparison give uniform fixed-list bounds for forward and inverse shuffles, with the stated substituted horizons.
- **claim-independent-shear-law** (construction, [source](#stmt-shear-lem-keys)): Fix an axis order $\boldsymbol\iota$ and a horizon $T\ge d$. Independently of the increments in <a href="#shear:eq:coordinate-chain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="shear:eq:coordinate-chain">[shear:eq:coordinate-chain]</a>, choose independent fair bits $b_{sj}$ for $j\ne i(s)$, put $b_{s,i(s)}=0$, and write $\Lambda$ for all these entries. Set $$S_s=I+e_{i(s)}b_s,\qquad A_0=I,\quad A_s=A_{s-1}S_s,\qquad v_s=A_{s-1}e_{i(s)},\qquad Z_s=A_sX^{\boldsymbol\iota}_s.$$ Conditional on $\Lambda$, the increments of $Z$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_s$. More precisely, if $\mathsf P_{v_1,\ldots,v_t}$ is the product-switch path law through time $t$ starting at the identity, then $$\label{shear:eq:prefix-factorization} \mathcal L((Z_s)_{s=0}^t\mid\Lambda) =\mathsf P_{v_1,\ldots,v_t}\qquad(0\le t\le T).$$ Every $d$ consecutive directions form a basis. For $s>d$, conditional on $v_1,\ldots,v_{s-1}$, the next direction is uniform on $V\setminus W_s$, with $W_s$ as in <a href="#shear:eq:hyperplane" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="shear:eq:hyperplane">[shear:eq:hyperplane]</a>. This law is unchanged by additionally observing any part of the virtual path prefix through time $s-1$.
  - Explanation: Independent shears realize fresh directions through deferred column reads and establish the conditional prefix law needed for terminal disclosure.
- **claim-omitted-label-reservoir** (bound, [source](#stmt-shear-prop-reservoir)): Let $d\ge3$, $b=N/8$, and $T=257d$. Let $B$ be a uniform $b$-subset of input labels, independent of the shuffle. For any fixed ordering of $V\setminus B$, let $\delta_T(B)$ be the distance of its image tuple under $X_T$ from uniform distinct positions. With $\theta=2^{1/4}(3/4)<1$, $$\label{shear:eq:reservoir-marginal} \mathbb E_B\delta_T(B) \le\frac{N^{3/2}}2 \left(e^{-8d}+2(N+1)\theta^{N/8}\right)^{1/2}=o(1).$$
  - Explanation: An independently sampled omitted reservoir provides unconditional balance and vanishing average complementary tuple error.
- **claim-averaged-input-tuples** (bound, [source](#stmt-lifts-averaged-tuples)): Fix an integer $b\ge2$ dividing $N$, put $m=N/b$, and let $t\ge d$ be an integer. Let $C$ be a uniformly sampled ordered tuple of $N-m$ distinct input labels, independent of the shuffle, and let $\mu_C$ be its time-$t$ physical image law with $C$ fixed. Then $$\label{lifts:averaged-bound} \mathbb E_C\\|\mu_C-\pi_{N-m}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le\frac{N^{3/2}}2 \left((1-1/(4b))^{t-d}+2\vartheta^{N/b}\right)^{1/2}, \qquad \vartheta=\frac32\,2^{-3/4}<1.$$ The same bound holds for a fixed ordered list of $N-m$ labels when its conditional distance is averaged over a uniformly chosen starting deck.
  - Explanation: Sampling the input tuple yields balance after a deterministic initial sweep. The result averages conditional distances, including those induced by a uniform starting deck.
- **claim-averaged-tuple-horizons** (bound, [source](#stmt-lifts-tuple-constants)): For $b=16$, the average distance in Lemma <a href="#lifts:averaged-tuples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lifts:averaged-tuples">22</a> at $t=513d$ is at most $$\varepsilon_N=\frac12N^{3/2} \left(N^{-8}+2\vartheta^{N/16}\right)^{1/2}=o(1).$$ At $t=2049d$ it is at most $N^{-10}$ for sufficiently large $d$. For $b=1024$ and $d\ge10$, time $t=(1+32b)d$ gives average distance $o(1)$.
  - Explanation: Substituting the stated omitted fractions and horizons gives vanishing average tuple errors and a stronger eventual polynomial bound.
- **claim-random-initial-basis-tuples** (bound, [source](#stmt-shear-prop-random-tuple)): Let $d\ge3$, $q=7N/8$, and $T=1024d$. For a uniformly sampled ordered distinct input tuple $C$, independent of $X$, $$\label{shear:eq:random-tuple} \mathbb E_C\\|\mathcal L(X_T(C)\mid C)-\pi_q\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le\frac{q\sqrt N}{2} \left(e^{-16d}+2(N+1)e^{-N/256}\right)^{1/2}=o(1).$$ The corresponding hidden-card energy is $O(N^{-20})$, uniformly over its index.
  - Explanation: A random initial basis permits contraction from the start, while input averaging removes the initial frame relabeling.
- **claim-ordinary-random-input-law** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-average-law)): Let $d\ge6$, $b=64$, $q=N(1-1/b)$, and $T=2048d$. The average, over uniform ordered lists of $q$ distinct input labels, of their time-$T$ physical image-law distance from $\pi_q$ is $o(1)$.
  - Explanation: Nearly complete image laws converge after averaging uniformly sampled input lists.
- **claim-delayed-half-space-symmetry** (bound, [source](#stmt-shear-lem-delayed-symmetry)): Let $V=\mathbb F_2^d$, $N=2^d$, and fix a coordinate $i$. Let $j_1,\ldots,j_{d-1}$ list all coordinates other than $i$, in any order. Condition on a sigma field $\mathcal A$ which specifies a nonempty set $B_0\subseteq V$ of size $h$ and a probability vector $p_0$ supported on $B_0$. Independently of $\mathcal A$, let $S_r$ be independent uniform matching switches in direction $e_{j_r}$, for $1\le r\le d-1$. Define $B_r=S_rB_{r-1}$. On each matching edge, obtain $p_r$ by averaging the two masses when both endpoints belong to $B_{r-1}$, and by moving the mass with its unique available endpoint when exactly one endpoint belongs to $B_{r-1}$; it is zero on edges with no available endpoint. Put $$a_r(x)=p_r(x)-h^{-1}\mathbf1_{B_r}(x),\qquad E_r=\sum_{x\in V}a_r(x)^2, \qquad H_b=\{x\in V:x_i=b\}\quad(b=0,1).$$ For each $z\in V$ with $z_i=0$, let $T_zx=x+x_i z$. Conditional on $\mathcal A$, the terminal pair $(B_{d-1},a_{d-1})$ has the same law as $$\bigl(T_zB_{d-1},\ a_{d-1}\circ T_z^{-1}\bigr). \label{shear:eq:delayed-symmetry-invariance}$$ The counts $h_b=\|B_r\cap H_b\|$ are independent of $r$ and are $\mathcal A$-measurable. If a further independent fair matching switch in direction $e_i$ produces the energy $E_d$ by the same rule, then $$\mathbb E[E_{d-1}-E_d\mid\mathcal A] \ge\frac{\min(h_0,h_1)}N\, \mathbb E[E_{d-1}\mid\mathcal A]. \label{shear:eq:delayed-symmetry-loss}$$ The statement includes $d=1$, when the intermediate schedule is empty.
  - Explanation: Intervening coordinate layers create conditional half-space translation symmetry. The later coordinate switch contracts energy according to the earlier fixed half-counts.
- **claim-degree-and-retained-coset-inputs** (construction, [source](#stmt-shear-prop-fourier-inputs)): For every fixed $u>0$, $C_u<\infty$ and $Z_u(s)\to0$ as $s\to\infty$.* *Let $H_1,\ldots,H_r$ be subgroups of a finite group $G$, and let $\mu$ be a probability measure. Write $\mu_H$ for its image on $G/H$. If $$\delta=\sum_{i=1}^r \\|\mu_{H_i}-U_{G/H_i}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}},$$ there is a measure $0\le f\le\mu$, of mass $z\ge1-\delta$, with $$f(gH_i)\le [G:H_i]^{-1}\qquad(g\in G,\ 1\le i\le r).$$ It can be obtained by successive trimming of excess coset mass or by taking the pointwise minimum of the separately trimmed measures. When $z>0$, its normalization is within $1-z$ of $\mu$ in TV and has coset cap $z^{-1}$ times uniform.* *Alternatively, delete the union of all cosets whose original $\mu$-mass exceeds twice uniform. The deleted mass is $\eta\le2\delta$. If $\delta<1/2$, the normalized retained law is exactly $\eta$ away from $\mu$ in TV and has coset cap $2/(1-\eta)$ times uniform.
  - Explanation: All fixed positive inverse-degree powers are summable. Common retained measures can cap every specified coset system, with explicit normalization or deletion costs.
- **claim-disjoint-support-transfers** (bound, [source](#stmt-shear-prop-disjoint-transfer)): Let $X$ have $N$ elements and $G=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(X)$. Let $M_1,\ldots,M_r$ be disjoint nonempty subsets of $X$, where $r\ge1$, and put $H_i=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(M_i)$, fixing the complement pointwise. Their union need not be $X$. Suppose $f\ge0$ has mass $z\le1$ and $$f(gH_i)\le K[G:H_i]^{-1}\qquad(g\in G,\ 1\le i\le r)$$ for some $K\ge0$. For an irreducible unitary representation $\rho$ of degree $D$, set $$S_i(D)= \sum_{\substack{\tau\in\widehat H_i\\ \dim\tau\le D^{1/r}}} (\dim\tau)^2.$$ For every $u>0$, the separate orbit and isotypic estimates are $$\begin{aligned} \\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} &\le \left(\frac{zK}{D}\sum_iS_i(D)\right)^{1/2} \le \sqrt{zKrC_u}\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2r)}, \label{shear:eq:noncovering-orbit-general}\\ \\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 &\le \frac{zK}{D}\sum_iS_i(D) \le zKrC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/r}. \label{shear:eq:noncovering-isotypic-general} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, when $r>4$, the inverse-square orbit estimate gives $$\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le\sqrt{rKC_2}\,D^{-(1-4/r)/2}. \label{shear:eq:orbit-bound}$$ A separate coefficient-projection argument gives $$\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le\frac{K^2}{D}\sum_{i=1}^r \sum_{\substack{\tau\in\widehat H_i\\ \dim\tau\le D^{1/r}}}(\dim\tau)^2 \le K^2rC_{10}D^{-1+12/r}. \label{shear:eq:hs-general}$$ Finally, for $\rho=\rho_\lambda$ with $\lambda\vdash N$, put $$k_\lambda=N-\max(\lambda_1,\lambda'_1), \qquad M(k)=\sum_{j=0}^k p(j),$$ where $p(j)$ is the partition number and $p(0)=1$. The branching refinement is $$\\|\widehat f(\rho_\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le zKrM(k_\lambda)D^{-1+2/r}. \label{shear:eq:hs-branching}$$ All these statements allow unequal support sizes and unrestricted restriction multiplicities. In particular they include the probability-law cases $z=1$.
  - Explanation: Coset caps on disjoint supports give orbit, isotypic, coefficient, and branching estimates. Coverage of every label and restrictions on multiplicity are unnecessary.
- **claim-retained-mass-normalization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-subprobability-fourier)): For a complex mass function $a$ on a finite group $G$, $$\sum_\rho D_\rho\\|\widehat a(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 =\|G\|\sum_g\|a(g)\|^2. \label{shear:eq:plancherel-mass}$$ If $\xi\ge0$ has mass $m\le1$, its transform is not divided by $m$, and $$\begin{aligned} \|G\|\sum_g\left\|\xi(g)-\frac{m}{\|G\|}\right\|^2 &=\sum_{\rho\ne\mathrm{triv}}D_\rho \\|\widehat\xi(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2, \label{eq:subprobability-centered}\\ \left(\sum_g\|\xi(g)-U_G(g)\|\right)^2 &\le(1-m)^2+\sum_{\rho\ne\mathrm{triv}}D_\rho \\|\widehat\xi(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2. \label{eq:subprobability-full} \end{aligned}$$ If $\xi\le\mu$ for a probability measure $\mu$, then $\sum_g\|\mu(g)-\xi(g)\|=1-m$. When $m>0$, $\\|\xi/m-\mu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le1-m$.
  - Explanation: Plancherel for complex masses separates missing mass from nonlinear Fourier contributions and controls normalization of dominated retained measures.
- **claim-isotypic-two-block-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shear-cor-512)): For the Thorp shuffle on $N=2^d$ cards, $$\\|q_d^{*(512d)}-U_G\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0 \qquad(d\longrightarrow\infty).$$ The convergence is uniform over deterministic initial orderings.
  - Explanation: The fixed-list marginal estimate and companion isotypic transfer yield the strongest explicit mixing horizon stated here.
- **claim-orbit-eight-block-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shear-thm-2048)): For the physical shuffle on $N=2^d$ labeled cards, $$\\|q_d^{*(2048d)}-U_G\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1). \label{shear:eq:2048}$$ The fixed-list estimate at $257d$ also gives a proof using eight time blocks at $2056d$ with a retained subprobability. The random omitted set estimate gives another proof at $2056d$ using normalized deletion. All three conclusions hold for every deterministic starting deck.
  - Explanation: Orbit contraction gives the main full-deck horizon and separate retained-mass and normalized-deletion alternatives.
- **claim-averaged-marginal-retention** (construction, [source](#stmt-frames-average-marginals)): Let $d\ge6$, $b=64$, $q=N(1-1/b)$, $T=2048d$, and $\mu_T=q_d^{*T}$. The average over uniform ordered lists of $q$ distinct input labels of the total-variation distance of their outputs from a uniform injection is $o(1)$. There is a subprobability $f\le\mu_T$ of mass $1-o(1)$ and a partition into 64 equal parts for which every complementary marginal of $f$ is bounded above by the corresponding uniform marginal. Consequently, $$\\|q_d^{*(32768d)}-U_G\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1).$$ The full-deck conclusion holds for every deterministic starting deck.
  - Explanation: Averaged list errors select a partition admitting common marginal domination, which then amplifies to full-deck convergence.
- **claim-coefficient-block-amplification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shear-thm-32-block)): The fixed-list marginal bound at $T=2049d$ has a separate Hilbert–Schmidt amplification proving $$\\|q_d^{*(65568d)}-U_G\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1).$$
  - Explanation: A separate coefficient projection estimate amplifies the fixed-list marginal bound through the stated independent time blocks.
- **claim-branching-block-amplification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shear-thm-4096)): The averaged-input estimate of Proposition <a href="#shear:prop:random-tuple" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shear:prop:random-tuple">24</a> has an amplification proving $$\\|q_d^{*(4096d)}-U_G\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1).$$
  - Explanation: The branching refinement amplifies the averaged-input estimate to full-permutation convergence.
- **claim-optimal-mixing-order** (bound, [source](#stmt-shear-cor-order)): For all sufficiently large $d$, $$d<t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\le512d.$$ Thus the mixing time has order $\Theta(d)$ in physical shuffles. The one-card support bound also gives $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\ge d$ for every $d\ge1$.
  - Explanation: The upper horizon and support obstructions establish the optimal linear order of physical mixing time.
- **claim-matching-label-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-matching-invariance)): Fix an integer $T\ge d$ and directions $v_1,\ldots,v_T$ such that every consecutive $d$ form a basis. There are invertible linear maps $L_0,\ldots,L_T$ with $L_0e_i=v_i$ for $1\le i\le d$ such that, if $g_t$ is the cyclic-layer process, then $L_tg_tL_0^{-1}$ is the independent switch process with directions $v_t$. This assertion is an equality in law conditional on the entire fixed sequence. If $L_0=I$, the matching on trajectory labels before each switch is unchanged. For general $L_0$, the same statement holds after relabeling the initial labels by $L_0$. Consequently the average, over uniform ordered input lists of any fixed length, of the endpoint distance to a uniform injection is unchanged.
  - Explanation: The fixed-schedule frames preserve trajectory-label matchings after the appropriate input relabeling and preserve averaged endpoint distances.
- **claim-complete-path-hole-flow** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-holes)): Fix an integer $T\ge1$ and nonzero directions $v_1,\ldots,v_T$. Let $Z_0=I$ and $Z_t=\xi_tZ_{t-1}$, where the $\xi_t$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_t$. Fix complete feasible trajectories of some observed labels. If $m\ge1$ labels remain unobserved, let $F_t$ be the positions not occupied by the observed labels at time $t$. For a specified unobserved label, let $p_t$ be its conditional position law given those complete observed trajectories.* *The vector $p_t$ evolves from its initial point mass by averaging on pairs with two positions in $F_{t-1}$ and deterministic transport on pairs with one such position. It depends only on the directions and observed paths through time $t$. The uniform vector $1/m$ is transported to the uniform vector on $F_t$. Thus, with $E_t=\sum_{x\in F_t}(p_t(x)-1/m)^2$, one has $0\le E_t\le1$ and $$E_{t-1}-E_t=\frac12\sum_{\{x,y\}\subset F_{t-1}\text{ paired at }t} (p_{t-1}(x)-p_{t-1}(y))^2. \label{frames:hole-loss}$$
  - Explanation: Observed complete trajectories fix only their visited time-edge coins. The remaining hidden mass obeys the same forward averaging and transport recursion.
- **claim-conditioning-interface** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-conditional-interface)): Fix integers $T\ge1$ and $1\le q\le n$. Let $\mathbf v=(v_1,\ldots,v_T)$ be a random schedule of nonzero directions. Set $Z_0=I$, and suppose that, conditional on $\mathbf v$, the increments of $Z$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_1,\ldots,v_T$. Let $C=(c_1,\ldots,c_q)$ be a deterministic list of distinct labels, or a random such list independent of the joint process $(\mathbf v,(Z_t)_{t=0}^T)$. For $1\le j\le q$, put $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal F_t^{C,j} &=\sigma\bigl(C,v_1,\ldots,v_t,\, Z_s(c_i):0\le s\le t,\ i<j\bigr), \label{frames:online-field}\\ \mathcal H^{C,j} &=\sigma\bigl(C,\mathbf v,\, Z_s(c_i):0\le s\le T,\ i<j\bigr). \label{frames:terminal-field} \end{aligned}$$ For $0\le t\le T$, define $p_t(x)=\Pr\{Z_t(c_j)=x\mid\mathcal F_t^{C,j}\}$. Then, for every $x\in V$, $$\Pr\{Z_t(c_j)=x\mid\mathcal H^{C,j}\}=p_t(x) \quad\text{almost surely}. \label{frames:full-path-prefix}$$ For $t<T$, let $K_t(v)=\Pr\{v_{t+1}=v\mid v_1,\ldots,v_t\}$. Then $$\Pr\{Z_t(c_j)=x,\ v_{t+1}=v\mid\mathcal F_t^{C,j}\} =p_t(x)K_t(v). \label{frames:direction-factorization}$$
  - Explanation: Complete predecessor paths and chronological prefixes give the same hidden mass. Future directions remain factorized in the smaller prefix field.
- **claim-centered-cross-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-cross-energy)): Suppose, given the generated past, the next direction is uniform outside a hyperplane $J\subset V$. Split the $m$ unobserved positions into the two cosets, denoting the resulting sets by $F_0,F_1$ and their sizes by $m_0,m_1$. For any centered real mass vector $z$ known from that past, the expected squared-norm loss obtained by averaging on the paired unobserved positions is $$\frac1n\sum_{x\in F_0,\ y\in F_1}(z_x-z_y)^2 \ge \frac{\min(m_0,m_1)}n\sum_xz_x^2.$$ If the next direction is uniform outside a smaller subspace contained in $J$, the same lower bound holds with $1/(2n)$ in place of $1/n$.
  - Explanation: Fresh cross-hyperplane matchings contract any centered real vector whose averaging rule is specified.
- **claim-earlier-crossing-recurrence** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-crossing-balance)): Fix integers $T\ge d$ and $r\ge1$. For any fixed observed labels and any $d<t\le T$, if $m$ positions remain unobserved, then $$\Pr\{\min(\|F_{t-1}\cap J_t\|,\|F_{t-1}\setminus J_t\|)<m/4\} \le2e^{-m/8}.$$ For the hidden-card energy of Lemma <a href="#frames:holes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:holes">38</a>, interpreted in this random process by Corollary <a href="#frames:conditional-interface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:conditional-interface">39</a>, if $m\ge n/r$ and $\alpha=1/(4r)$, then $$\mathbb E E_T\le(1-\alpha)^{T-d}+2e^{-n/(8r)}. \label{frames:crossing-recursion}$$
  - Explanation: An earlier crossing supplies unconditional balance and an expected energy recurrence for fixed observed labels.
- **claim-descending-history-removal** (construction, [source](#stmt-frames-backward-removal)): Let $T\ge d$ be an integer, and let $r\ge2$ divide $n$. Partition $V$ into equal input blocks $M_1,\ldots,M_r$ and, for each $i$, fix an order $c_{i,1},\ldots,c_{i,q}$ on its complement, where $q=n-n/r$. Write $\mathcal P$ for the partition together with these orders, and let $K_i=\operatorname{Sym}(M_i)$ fix the complement pointwise. Use the variable-direction process just defined, whose first frame is $L_0=I$. For $1\le j\le q$, let $$\mathcal H_{i,j} =\sigma\bigl(\mathbf v,\, Z_s(c_{i,h}):0\le s\le T,\ h<j\bigr),\qquad F_{i,j}=V\setminus\{Z_T(c_{i,h}):h<j\},$$ and define $$p_{i,j}(x)=\Pr\{Z_T(c_{i,j})=x\mid\mathcal H_{i,j}\},\qquad E_{i,j}=\sum_{x\in F_{i,j}} \left(p_{i,j}(x)-\frac1{n-j+1}\right)^2.$$ Suppose $\mathbb E E_{i,j}\le\varepsilon_n$ uniformly. Put $\mathcal G_{\mathcal P}=\bigcap_{i,j}\{E_{i,j}\le n^{-6}\}$, and let $\mu$ be the original cyclic endpoint law at time $T$. Then $\Pr(\mathcal G_{\mathcal P}^c)\le rq n^6\varepsilon_n$. The measure $$\nu(g)=\Pr\{\mathcal G_{\mathcal P},\ L_T^{-1}Z_T=g\}$$ satisfies $0\le\nu\le\mu$ and, for large $n$, $\nu(gK_i)\le2U_G(gK_i)$ for every $g\in G$ and $1\le i\le r$. If $a=\Pr(\mathcal G_{\mathcal P})=1-o(1)$, its normalized law $\bar\nu=\nu/a$ satisfies $\bar\nu(gK_i)\le4U_G(gK_i)$ and $\\|\bar\nu-\mu\\|_{\rm TV}\le1-a=o(1)$.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous energy constraints for chosen complementary orders define a retained event. Removing those constraints in descending order gives pointwise coset caps.
- **claim-retained-event-applications** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-pointwise-applications)): The physical shuffle has total-variation distance $o(1)$ from uniform by time $4096d$. The choices $r=32$, $T=2561d$ give the separate subprobability construction of Lemma <a href="#frames:backward-removal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:backward-removal">42</a>, and sixteen such blocks give distance $o(1)$ at time $40976d$.
  - Explanation: The common retained event yields separate full-deck mixing applications with different partitions and block lengths.
- **claim-stopped-fixed-list-law** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-stopped-marginals)): Let $d\ge4$. Put $b=16$, $m=n/b$, $q=n-m$, and $T=(2+20b)d=322d$. For every fixed ordered list of $q$ distinct input positions, its image under the physical shuffle at time $T$ has distance at most $$\delta_d=\frac{4T}{m}+\frac12n^{3/2} (1-1/(4b))^{(T-2d+1)/2}=o(1) \label{frames:stopped-delta}$$ from a uniform injection, uniformly over the chosen list.
  - Explanation: Nested balance events allow the whole list to pay the exceptional imbalance cost only once.
- **claim-stopped-pruning-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-pruning-application)): The stopped partial-observation estimate and spectral pruning give full-permutation distance $o(1)$ by time $5152d$.
  - Explanation: The stopped fixed-marginal estimate supplies the hypotheses for companion spectral pruning and subsequent convolution amplification.
- **claim-reference-row-flow** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-reference-rows)): Fix a realized cyclic history $g=(g_0,\ldots,g_T)$ and a nonempty set $F$ of labels. For $u\in F$, let $p_t^{u,F}(x)$ be the conditional probability under $\mathbb P$ that card $u$ is at $x$ at time $t$, given the complete trajectories of the labels in $V\setminus F$, evaluated on the trajectories supplied by $g$. Define a row indexed by $F$ by $$r_t^{u,F}(v)=p_t^{u,F}(g_t(v)),\qquad v\in F.$$ Then, for this realized history, $$r_0^{u,F}=e_u^{\mathsf T},\qquad r_t^{u,F}=r_{t-1}^{u,F}B_t^F, \qquad r_t^{u,F}=e_u^{\mathsf T}Q_t^F. \label{frames:reference-row-flow}$$ Here $e_u$ is the coordinate vector in $\mathbb R^F$. In particular the conditional likelihood of the realized endpoint of card $u$ is $$p_T^{u,F}(g_T(u))=Q_T^F(u,u). \label{frames:diagonal-shadow}$$ The conditioning in this statement remains on the paths of $V\setminus F$; the realized history enters $r_t^{u,F}$ through the indexing bijection $v\mapsto g_t(v)$.
  - Explanation: Trajectory-label coordinates express hidden conditional probabilities as rows of products of averaging matrices, including the realized endpoint likelihood.
- **claim-common-typical-history** (construction, [source](#stmt-frames-typical-history)): Define the event on cyclic histories by $$\label{frames:typical-average} \mathcal G_{\mathrm{ent}} =\bigcap_{m=n/L}^{n} \left\{g:\mathbb E_{F:\,\|F\|=m}Z_T^F(g)\le n^{-8}\right\},$$ where each average is uniform over subsets of labels. Then $\mathbb P(\mathcal G_{\mathrm{ent}})=1-o(1)$.
  - Explanation: A common history event controls subset-averaged matrix energy simultaneously over the required reservoir sizes and has asymptotically full probability.
- **claim-all-tilt-shadow-entropy** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-shadow-entropy)): For every probability law $\nu$ on histories supported on $\mathcal G_{\mathrm{ent}}$, $$\label{frames:entropy-comparison} D(\nu\\|\mathbb P)\ge \mathbb E_{X:\,\|X\|=q}D(\nu_X\\|U_X)-n^{-2}.$$
  - Explanation: The entropy comparison holds for every history law supported on the common event, including coefficient-selected tilted laws.
- **claim-entropy-coefficient-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-entropy-tail)): There is an absolute $C$ such that for every irreducible unitary representation $\rho$ of $G$, of degree $D$, and unit vectors $u,z$, $$\mathbb P\{\mathcal G_{\mathrm{ent}}, \|\langle z,\rho(g_T)u\rangle\|\ge D^{-1/8}\} \le C D^{-1/(2L)}. \label{frames:coefficient-tail}$$ Put $a=\mathbb P(\mathcal G_{\mathrm{ent}})$. When $a>0$, the conditional endpoint law $\eta=\mathcal L(g_T\mid\mathcal G_{\mathrm{ent}})$ is defined and satisfies $$\\|\widehat\eta(\rho)\\|_{\rm op} \le D^{-1/8}+\frac{C}{a}D^{-1/(2L)}.$$ For all sufficiently large $d$, Lemma <a href="#frames:typical-history" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:typical-history">47</a> gives $a\ge1/2$. In that range the last bound is at most $C'D^{-1/(2L)}$ for the absolute constant $C'=1+2C$.
  - Explanation: The all-tilt comparison produces representation-coefficient tails and operator contraction for the conditional endpoint law.
- **claim-entropy-history-amplification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-entropy-application)): With $L=128$, $T=(1+100L)d$, and $b=32L$, the shuffle has full-permutation distance $o(1)$ at time $bT$.
  - Explanation: The common typical-history law amplifies to full-deck mixing after accounting for parity and the cost of restoring removed histories.
- **claim-deferred-column-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-aux-theorem)): For the Thorp shuffle on $n=2^d$ labeled cards, $$\max_{\sigma\in S_n} \bigl\\|P_d^{512040d}(\sigma,\cdot)-U_n\bigr\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} =o(1)\qquad(d\longrightarrow\infty),$$ where $U_n$ is the uniform probability measure on $S_n$ and one time step is one physical shuffle. Consequently, at total-variation threshold $1/4$, $$d\le t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\le512040d$$ for all sufficiently large integers $d$.
  - Explanation: Deferred shear columns, averaged input ordering, and a single-vector orbit bound prove a separate uniform full-deck horizon.
- **claim-inverse-shear-conditional-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-aux-directions)): Conditional on $\Lambda$, the increments of $X$ are independent uniform switches in the coordinate directions $e_{\iota_i}$. For every $t\le T$, the conditional law of $(Y_i)_{i=0}^t$ given $\Lambda$ is the product-switch law determined by $w_1,\ldots,w_t$.* *Every $d$ consecutive directions $w_i$ form a basis. At layer $i$, learning $w_i$ does not change the conditional law of the preceding virtual positions given $w_1,\ldots,w_{i-1}$ and any specified virtual path prefixes through layer $i-1$. For $i>d$, that direction is uniform outside $$\operatorname{span}(w_{i-d+1},\ldots,w_{i-1})$$ conditional on that same direction and path-prefix field. Finally, conditional on the complete direction sequence $W$, disclosing the remaining information in $\Lambda$ does not change the conditional law of the virtual trajectories, including after a fixed collection of those trajectories has been observed.
  - Explanation: The inverse-frame formulation gives coordinate switches and virtual prefix laws, preserving fresh directions before terminal shear disclosure.
- **claim-auxiliary-energy-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-aux-energy)): Uniformly for $0\le j<k$, with expectation over the ordering and shuffle construction, $$\label{frames:aux:energy-bound} \mathbb E E_T\le e^{-25d}+T\delta_n, \qquad \delta_n=2n(n+1)\exp\left(-\frac{n}{16r}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Input-order averaging and simultaneous affine half-space balance yield a uniform expected hidden-card energy bound.
- **claim-auxiliary-average-marginal** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-aux-marginal)): Let $C=(a_1,\ldots,a_k)$ be a uniformly chosen ordered list of distinct initial cards, independent of $X_T\sim\mu$, and let $\pi_k$ be uniform on ordered $k$-tuples of distinct positions. Then $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_C\bigl\\|\mathcal L(X_T(C)\mid C)-\pi_k\bigr\\|_{\rm TV} &\le\varepsilon_n,\notag\\ \varepsilon_n &=\frac{k}{2}\sqrt{n\bigl(e^{-25d}+T\delta_n\bigr)}=o(1). \label{frames:aux:marginal-error} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Terminal disclosure and same-input endpoint comparison convert the auxiliary energy estimate into averaged tuple error.
- **claim-auxiliary-orbit-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-aux-orbit-lift)): If $\rho$ is an irreducible unitary representation of $G=S_n$ of dimension $D$, then the measure in <a href="#frames:aux:cosets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="frames:aux:cosets">[frames:aux:cosets]</a> satisfies $$\label{frames:aux:operator-bound} \\|\widehat\nu(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le C_rD^{-1/2+11/r},\qquad C_r=\sqrt{2rK_0}.$$
  - Explanation: The common trimmed coset law contracts every irreducible Fourier matrix through an inverse-degree orbit estimate.
- **claim-four-block-random-input-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lifts-random-four-blocks)): As $d\to\infty$, with $n=2^d$, $$\max_{g_0\in G}\\|P_d^{2052d}(g_0,\cdot)-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} =\\|q_d^{*(2052d)}-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: An averaged-input partition, common trimming, and isotypic contraction prove convergence of the full permutation law.
- **claim-sixteen-equivariant-kernels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lifts-sixteen-theorem)): As $d\to\infty$, with $n=2^d$, $$\max_{g_0\in G}\\|P_d^{32784d}(g_0,\cdot)-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} =\\|q_d^{*(32784d)}-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Retained equivariant quotient kernels amplify averaged marginals after controlling the entire sign multiplicity space.
- **claim-overlapping-omitted-set-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lifts-neumann-theorem)): As $d\to\infty$, with $n=2^d$, $$\max_{g_0\in G}\\|P_d^{262152d}(g_0,\cdot)-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} =\\|q_d^{*(262152d)}-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: The omitted-set transfer uses the average over overlapping omitted sets and preserves the order of their projection products.
- **claim-capped-preimage-character-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-char-main)): Let $n=2^d$, and count one physical Thorp shuffle as one time step, using $n/2$ independent fair switches. For all sufficiently large integers $d$, the worst-initial-deck total-variation mixing time at threshold $1/4$ satisfies $$d\le t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\le 6060000d.$$ In fact, the worst-initial-deck distance at time $60000(101d-1)$ tends to zero as $d\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Capped half-deck preimages and character smoothing yield an explicit uniform physical mixing horizon.
- **claim-half-list-uniformity** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-char-half-list)): Let $\pi$ be the product of $L=100d$ consecutive layers in a periodic coordinate order on $n=2^d$ positions. Uniformly over ordered lists of $q\le n/2$ distinct inputs, the law of their images under $\pi$ has total-variation distance $o(1)$ from the uniform ordered injection. The same assertion holds for $\pi^{-1}$. Both assertions are uniform in the starting phase and the order of the coordinate axes.
  - Explanation: The shared half-list estimate holds for forward and inverse coordinate segments, uniformly over phase and axis order.
- **claim-one-pass-character-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-char-one-pass)): Let $b=2^h$ and let $Y$ apply each of the $h$ coordinate layers once, in any order, using independent fair switches. For every irreducible representation $\rho_\alpha$ of $S_b$, put $T_\alpha=\mathbb E\rho_\alpha(Y)$. Uniformly for all sufficiently large $b$, $$\frac{\operatorname{tr}(T_\alpha^*T_\alpha)}{D_\alpha} =\frac{\operatorname{tr}(T_\alpha T_\alpha^*)}{D_\alpha} \le2D_\alpha^{-1/100}.$$
  - Explanation: Unique-route fixed-point tails and character bounds control the normalized trace of a single coordinate pass.
- **claim-branching-sweep-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-char-sweep-trace)): Let $s=2^\ell$ and let $G_s$ be a sweep using each of the $\ell$ coordinate directions once, in any order, with independent fair switches. There are absolute $a>0,C_1<\infty$ such that, for every sufficiently large $s$ and every complex irreducible representation $\rho_\alpha$ of $S_s$, $$q_\alpha:=D_\alpha^{-1} \left\\|\mathbb E\rho_\alpha(G_s)\right\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le C_1D_\alpha^{-a}.$$ The Hilbert–Schmidt norm uses the ordinary, unnormalized trace.
  - Explanation: A distinct restriction argument bounds the same sweep trace using the number of branching constituents.
- **claim-nonlinear-minimum-degree** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-char-minimum-degree)): For even $n\ge6$, every irreducible representation of $S_n$ other than trivial and sign has degree at least $n/2$.
  - Explanation: Matching transpositions and their reordered characters force a dimension lower bound outside the trivial and sign types.
- **claim-modified-character-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-char-block-norm)): There is an absolute $C_0$ such that, for every sufficiently large $n=2^d$, every modified block above, and every irreducible $\rho$ of $S_n$ of degree $D$ other than trivial or sign, $$\\|\mathbb E\rho(\sigma\pi)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le C_0D^{-1/20000}.$$ The modified block has zero sign coefficient. These statements are uniform in its starting phase.
  - Explanation: Separate capped preimage marginals control positive trace tests and yield uniform block contraction with exact parity cancellation.
- **claim-repaired-half-deck-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-char-main)): There are absolute positive integers $C,K$ such that the Thorp shuffle on $n=2^d$ cards has worst-start total-variation distance tending to zero from uniform after $$K\bigl((d-1)+Cd\bigr)$$ physical shuffles. For every fixed ordered list of $n/2$ distinct input positions, its image after $Cd$ consecutive coordinate layers is within $o(1)$ of the uniform injection, uniformly in the input list and the starting phase of the coordinate cycle.
  - Explanation: Exact repaired half-deck marginals and character amplification give uniform full-deck convergence with symbolic absolute block constants.
- **claim-simultaneous-half-marginal-repair** (construction, [source](#stmt-char-repair)): Partition a set of size $2s$ into two ordered sets $J_1,J_2$, each of size $s$. Let $A_0$ be a random permutation. Suppose the ordered image of $J_i$ under $A_0$ has total-variation distance $\epsilon_i$ from a uniform injection. Let $\epsilon_{\mathrm{part}}$ be the distance of the ordered output partition $(A_0J_1,A_0J_2)$ from its uniform law. There is a permutation law $A$ whose two ordered image marginals are exactly uniform and such that $$\label{char:repaircost} \\|\mathcal L(A)-\mathcal L(A_0)\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le\epsilon_1+\epsilon_2+3\epsilon_{\mathrm{part}}.$$ Conditional on its output partition, each of the two bijections is uniform. Their joint law may be dependent.
  - Explanation: Conditional couplings repair both image marginals and the output partition at bounded total-variation cost while allowing dependence between the repaired bijections.

```json
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-one-card-lower",
        "exact_quote": "For a fixed initial card and an integer $0\\le t<d$, its location law has support at most $2^t$.         "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-general-frame",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:frames\" label=\"lem:frames\"></span>Let $T\\ge d$, and let $\\boldsymbol\\iota=(\\iota_1,\\ldots,\\iota_d)$ be a permutation of $1,\\ldots,d$. Write $i(t)=\\iota_{1+((t-1)\\bmod d)}$, and let $X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_0=\\mathrm{id}$ and $X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_t=Q^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_t\n X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_{t-1}$, where the increments are independent and uniform in $C_{e_{i(t)}}$.*\n\n\n*Fix directions $v_1,\\ldots,v_T$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:frames:bases}\n (v_s,\\ldots,v_{s+d-1})\\text{ is a basis of }V\n \\qquad(1\\le s\\le T-d+1).$$ Let $Z_0=\\mathrm{id}$, and let $Z_t$ evolve by independent uniform switches in these directions. There are linear bijections $L_0,\\ldots,L_T$, determined by the fixed axis order and direction schedule, such that $$\\label{shear:eq:general-frame}\n (Z_t)_{t=0}^T\\overset{\\mathrm{law}}=\n \\bigl(L_tX^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_tL_0^{-1}\\bigr)_{t=0}^T,\n \\qquad L_0e_{\\iota_j}=v_j\\quad(1\\le j\\le d).$$ In particular, if $\\boldsymbol\\iota=(1,\\ldots,d)$ and $v_j=e_j$ for $1\\le j\\le d$, then $L_0=I$ and $$\\label{eq:frames:endpoint}\n Z_T\\overset{\\mathrm{law}}=L_TX_T=L_TR^{-T}Y_T.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-frames-marginals",
        "exact_quote": "Under Lemma <a href=\"#lem:frames\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:frames\">[lem:frames]</a>, let $0\\le q\\le n$ and let $\\pi_q$ be uniform on ordered distinct $q$-tuples of positions. For every fixed ordered list $C$ of $q$ distinct labels, $$\\label{eq:frames:tuple-input-map}\n \\|\\mathcal L(Z_T(C))-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n =\n \\|\\mathcal L(X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_T(L_0^{-1}C))-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}.$$ Thus the same-list distances agree when $L_0=I$. Their averages also agree when $C$ is sampled uniformly over ordered distinct lists, independently of the switch process: $$\\label{eq:frames:averaged-input-map}\n \\mathbb E_C\\|\\mathcal L(Z_T(C)\\mid C)-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n =\n \\mathbb E_C\\|\\mathcal L(X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_T(C)\\mid C)-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-frames-marginals",
          "exact_quote": "Under Lemma <a href=\"#lem:frames\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:frames\">[lem:frames]</a>, let $0\\le q\\le n$ and let $\\pi_q$ be uniform on ordered distinct $q$-tuples of positions. For every fixed ordered list $C$ of $q$ distinct labels, $$\\label{eq:frames:tuple-input-map}\n \\|\\mathcal L(Z_T(C))-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n =\n \\|\\mathcal L(X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_T(L_0^{-1}C))-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}.$$ Thus the same-list distances agree when $L_0=I$. Their averages also agree when $C$ is sampled uniformly over ordered distinct lists, independently of the switch process: $$\\label{eq:frames:averaged-input-map}\n \\mathbb E_C\\|\\mathcal L(Z_T(C)\\mid C)-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n =\n \\mathbb E_C\\|\\mathcal L(X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_T(C)\\mid C)-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-energy",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:marginal-averaging\" label=\"lem:marginal-averaging\"></span>In the preceding setup, for $0\\le t<T$ and $1\\le j\\le q$, $$\\label{eq:marginal-exact-decrement}\n \\Delta_{t+1}^j=\\Delta_t^j-\\frac12\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\{x,y\\}\\subseteq D_t^j\\\\y=x+v_{t+1}}}\n       \\bigl(p_t^j(x)-p_t^j(y)\\bigr)^2\n \\quad\\text{almost surely},$$ where each unordered matching edge is counted once. If $K_t$ is uniform on the complement of an $\\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable hyperplane $J_t$, then $$\\label{eq:marginal-cross-energy}\n \\mathbb E[\\Delta_t^j-\\Delta_{t+1}^j\\mid\\mathcal F_t^j]\n \\ge\n \\frac{\\min(|D_t^j\\cap J_t|,|D_t^j\\setminus J_t|)}{n}\\Delta_t^j.$$ More generally, suppose $K_t$ is uniform outside an $\\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable proper subspace $W_t$, and let $J_t$ be an $\\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable hyperplane containing $W_t$. Then the same right side with denominator $2n$ is a valid lower bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-energy",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:marginal-averaging\" label=\"lem:marginal-averaging\"></span>In the preceding setup, for $0\\le t<T$ and $1\\le j\\le q$, $$\\label{eq:marginal-exact-decrement}\n \\Delta_{t+1}^j=\\Delta_t^j-\\frac12\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\{x,y\\}\\subseteq D_t^j\\\\y=x+v_{t+1}}}\n       \\bigl(p_t^j(x)-p_t^j(y)\\bigr)^2\n \\quad\\text{almost surely},$$ where each unordered matching edge is counted once. If $K_t$ is uniform on the complement of an $\\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable hyperplane $J_t$, then $$\\label{eq:marginal-cross-energy}\n \\mathbb E[\\Delta_t^j-\\Delta_{t+1}^j\\mid\\mathcal F_t^j]\n \\ge\n \\frac{\\min(|D_t^j\\cap J_t|,|D_t^j\\setminus J_t|)}{n}\\Delta_t^j.$$ More generally, suppose $K_t$ is uniform outside an $\\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable proper subspace $W_t$, and let $J_t$ be an $\\mathcal F_t^j$-measurable hyperplane containing $W_t$.         "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-tuple",
        "exact_quote": "In the preceding setup let $\\Lambda$ be terminal data that determine the complete direction schedule, and suppose $\\Xi$ is independent of $(\\Lambda,(Z_t)_{t=0}^T)$. For every $1\\le j\\le q$ and every $x\\in V$, assume that its online terminal vector is also the conditional hidden law after these data and the complete predecessor paths are known: $$\\label{eq:marginal-terminal-identification}\n \\Pr\\{Z_T(c_j)=x\\mid\n       \\Lambda,\\Xi,\\,\n       Z_s(c_i):0\\le s\\le T,\\ i<j\\}\n =p_T^j(x)\\quad\\text{almost surely}.$$ Then $$\\label{shear:eq:tuple-bound}\n \\mathbb E_{\\Lambda,\\Xi}\n \\|\\mathcal L(Z_T(C)\\mid\\Lambda,\\Xi)-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\le\\frac12\\sum_{j=1}^q\n       \\sqrt{(n-j+1)\\mathbb E\\Delta_T^j}.$$ All expectations are under the actual joint law of the data, list, and process. If $\\mathbb E\\Delta_T^j\\le a_T$ for every $j$, the right side is at most $q\\sqrt n\\,\\sqrt{a_T}/2$. For $q=0$ both sides are zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trim",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H_1,\\ldots,H_k$ be subgroups of a finite group $G$, and let $\\mu$ be a probability measure on $G$.         "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-lift-orbit-sum",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite set $X$ be partitioned into $k\\ge1$ nonempty sets $M_1,\\ldots,M_k$, and put $G=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(X)$. Let $H_a$ be the subgroup permuting $M_a$ and fixing $X\\setminus M_a$ pointwise. Suppose that a probability measure $\\nu$ on $G$ satisfies, for some $B\\ge 1$, $$\\label{eq:lift:coset-cap}\n  \\nu(gH_a)\\le B\\frac{|H_a|}{|G|}\n  \\qquad(g\\in G,\\ 1\\le a\\le k).$$ Then every irreducible complex unitary representation $\\rho$ of $G$, of dimension $D$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:lift:orbit-sum}\n  \\|\\widehat\\nu(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}^2\n  \\le \\frac{B}{D}\\sum_{a=1}^k\n       \\sum_{\\substack{\\pi\\in\\widehat H_a\\\\\\dim\\pi\\le D^{1/k}}}\n                    (\\dim\\pi)^2.$$ The block sizes need not be equal.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-concentration",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X_1,\\ldots,X_r$ be independent random variables in $[0,1]$ and put $S=\\sum_iX_i$. For $a\\ge0$ and $r\\ge1$, $$\\Pr(S-\\mathbb ES\\ge a),\\quad\\Pr(S-\\mathbb ES\\le-a)\\le e^{-2a^2/r}.$$ More generally, if $(M_j)_{j=0}^r$ is a real martingale whose increments have absolute value at most $c_j$, either tail at distance $a$ is at most $\\exp[-a^2/(2\\sum_jc_j^2)]$ when the denominator is positive. If all $c_j$ vanish, the martingale is constant. In particular, if $X$ counts the points of a uniform $m$-subset in a fixed half of a finite set of even size, then $$\\Pr(X<m/4)\\le e^{-m/32}\\qquad(m\\ge1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-concentration",

          "source_id": "PM-238.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-fixed-balance",
        "exact_quote": "Fix any $\\ell<N$ observed labels, and put $m=N-\\ell$. For $s>d$, let $D_{s-1}$ be their available positions just before step $s$.         "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-fixed-marginal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L\\ge2$ be a fixed integer dividing $N$, and let $0\\le q\\le N-N/L$. For every ordered list $C$ of $q$ distinct labels, every periodic ordering $\\boldsymbol\\iota$ of the standard axes, and every integer $T\\ge d$, $$\\label{shear:eq:general-fixed-marginal}\n \\|\\mathcal L(X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_T(C))-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\le\\frac{N^{3/2}}2\n \\left(e^{-(T-d)/(4L)}+2e^{-N/(8L)}\\right)^{1/2}.$$ The same bound holds for $(X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_T)^{-1}(C)$. For the physical shuffle and its inverse, the corresponding position laws satisfy the same bound.*\n\n\n\n\n*The following choices used below give distance at most $N^{3/2}\\sqrt{a_T}/2$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n L=8,\\quad T=256d:&\\quad\n a_T=(31/32)^{255d}+64e^{-N/64},\n \\label{shear:eq:256-marginal}\\\\\n L=8,\\quad T=257d:&\\quad\n a_T=e^{-8d}+2e^{-N/64},\n \\label{shear:eq:257-marginal}\\\\\n L=32,\\quad T=2049d:&\\quad\n a_T=e^{-16d}+256e^{-N/256}.\n \\label{shear:eq:2049-marginal}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Each of these three substituted bounds tends to zero as $d\\to\\infty$, uniformly in the list and axis order. The last energy bound is $o(N^{-4})$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-keys",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an axis order $\\boldsymbol\\iota$ and a horizon $T\\ge d$. Independently of the increments in <a href=\"#shear:eq:coordinate-chain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"shear:eq:coordinate-chain\">[shear:eq:coordinate-chain]</a>, choose independent fair bits $b_{sj}$ for $j\\ne i(s)$, put $b_{s,i(s)}=0$, and write $\\Lambda$ for all these entries. Set $$S_s=I+e_{i(s)}b_s,\\qquad\n A_0=I,\\quad A_s=A_{s-1}S_s,\\qquad\n v_s=A_{s-1}e_{i(s)},\\qquad\n Z_s=A_sX^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_s.$$ Conditional on $\\Lambda$, the increments of $Z$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_s$. More precisely, if $\\mathsf P_{v_1,\\ldots,v_t}$ is the product-switch path law through time $t$ starting at the identity, then $$\\label{shear:eq:prefix-factorization}\n \\mathcal L((Z_s)_{s=0}^t\\mid\\Lambda)\n =\\mathsf P_{v_1,\\ldots,v_t}\\qquad(0\\le t\\le T).$$ Every $d$ consecutive directions form a basis. For $s>d$, conditional on $v_1,\\ldots,v_{s-1}$, the next direction is uniform on $V\\setminus W_s$, with $W_s$ as in <a href=\"#shear:eq:hyperplane\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"shear:eq:hyperplane\">[shear:eq:hyperplane]</a>. This law is unchanged by additionally observing any part of the virtual path prefix through time $s-1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-keys",

Set $$S_s=I+e_{i(s)}b_s,\\qquad\n A_0=I,\\quad A_s=A_{s-1}S_s,\\qquad\n v_s=A_{s-1}e_{i(s)},\\qquad\n Z_s=A_sX^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_s.$$ Conditional on $\\Lambda$, the increments of $Z$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_s$. More precisely, if $\\mathsf P_{v_1,\\ldots,v_t}$ is the product-switch path law through time $t$ starting at the identity, then $$\\label{shear:eq:prefix-factorization}\n \\mathcal L((Z_s)_{s=0}^t\\mid\\Lambda)\n =\\mathsf P_{v_1,\\ldots,v_t}\\qquad(0\\le t\\le T).$$ Every $d$ consecutive directions form a basis. For $s>d$, conditional on $v_1,\\ldots,v_{s-1}$, the next direction is uniform on $V\\setminus W_s$, with $W_s$ as in <a href=\"#shear:eq:hyperplane\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"shear:eq:hyperplane\">[shear:eq:hyperplane]</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-reservoir",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge3$, $b=N/8$, and $T=257d$. Let $B$ be a uniform $b$-subset of input labels, independent of the shuffle. For any fixed ordering of $V\\setminus B$, let $\\delta_T(B)$ be the distance of its image tuple under $X_T$ from uniform distinct positions. With $\\theta=2^{1/4}(3/4)<1$, $$\\label{shear:eq:reservoir-marginal}\n \\mathbb E_B\\delta_T(B)\n \\le\\frac{N^{3/2}}2\n \\left(e^{-8d}+2(N+1)\\theta^{N/8}\\right)^{1/2}=o(1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lifts-averaged-tuples",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $b\\ge2$ dividing $N$, put $m=N/b$, and let $t\\ge d$ be an integer. Let $C$ be a uniformly sampled ordered tuple of $N-m$ distinct input labels, independent of the shuffle, and let $\\mu_C$ be its time-$t$ physical image law with $C$ fixed. Then $$\\label{lifts:averaged-bound}\n \\mathbb E_C\\|\\mu_C-\\pi_{N-m}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\le\\frac{N^{3/2}}2\n \\left((1-1/(4b))^{t-d}+2\\vartheta^{N/b}\\right)^{1/2},\n \\qquad \\vartheta=\\frac32\\,2^{-3/4}<1.$$ The same bound holds for a fixed ordered list of $N-m$ labels when its conditional distance is averaged over a uniformly chosen starting deck.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lifts-averaged-tuples",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $b\\ge2$ dividing $N$, put $m=N/b$, and let $t\\ge d$ be an integer.           "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix an integer $b\\ge2$ dividing $N$, put $m=N/b$, and let $t\\ge d$ be an integer.         "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge3$, $q=7N/8$, and $T=1024d$.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-random-tuple",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge3$, $q=7N/8$, and $T=1024d$.         }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-delayed-symmetry",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V=\\mathbb F_2^d$, $N=2^d$, and fix a coordinate $i$. Let $j_1,\\ldots,j_{d-1}$ list all coordinates other than $i$, in any order. Condition on a sigma field $\\mathcal A$ which specifies a nonempty set $B_0\\subseteq V$ of size $h$ and a probability vector $p_0$ supported on $B_0$. Independently of $\\mathcal A$, let $S_r$ be independent uniform matching switches in direction $e_{j_r}$, for $1\\le r\\le d-1$. Define $B_r=S_rB_{r-1}$. On each matching edge, obtain $p_r$ by averaging the two masses when both endpoints belong to $B_{r-1}$, and by moving the mass with its unique available endpoint when exactly one endpoint belongs to $B_{r-1}$; it is zero on edges with no available endpoint. Put $$a_r(x)=p_r(x)-h^{-1}\\mathbf1_{B_r}(x),\\qquad\n E_r=\\sum_{x\\in V}a_r(x)^2,\n \\qquad H_b=\\{x\\in V:x_i=b\\}\\quad(b=0,1).$$ For each $z\\in V$ with $z_i=0$, let $T_zx=x+x_i z$. Conditional on $\\mathcal A$, the terminal pair $(B_{d-1},a_{d-1})$ has the same law as $$\\bigl(T_zB_{d-1},\\ a_{d-1}\\circ T_z^{-1}\\bigr).\n \\label{shear:eq:delayed-symmetry-invariance}$$ The counts $h_b=|B_r\\cap H_b|$ are independent of $r$ and are $\\mathcal A$-measurable. If a further independent fair matching switch in direction $e_i$ produces the energy $E_d$ by the same rule, then $$\\mathbb E[E_{d-1}-E_d\\mid\\mathcal A]\n \\ge\\frac{\\min(h_0,h_1)}N\\,\n             \\mathbb E[E_{d-1}\\mid\\mathcal A].\n \\label{shear:eq:delayed-symmetry-loss}$$ The statement includes $d=1$, when the intermediate schedule is empty.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-delayed-symmetry",

Conditional on $\\mathcal A$, the terminal pair $(B_{d-1},a_{d-1})$ has the same law as $$\\bigl(T_zB_{d-1},\\ a_{d-1}\\circ T_z^{-1}\\bigr).\n \\label{shear:eq:delayed-symmetry-invariance}$$ The counts $h_b=|B_r\\cap H_b|$ are independent of $r$ and are $\\mathcal A$-measurable. If a further independent fair matching switch in direction $e_i$ produces the energy $E_d$ by the same rule, then $$\\mathbb E[E_{d-1}-E_d\\mid\\mathcal A]\n \\ge\\frac{\\min(h_0,h_1)}N\\,\n             \\mathbb E[E_{d-1}\\mid\\mathcal A].\n \\label{shear:eq:delayed-symmetry-loss}$$ The statement includes $d=1$, when the intermediate schedule is empty.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-fourier-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $u>0$, $C_u<\\infty$ and $Z_u(s)\\to0$ as $s\\to\\infty$.*\n\n*Let $H_1,\\ldots,H_r$ be subgroups of a finite group $G$, and let $\\mu$ be a probability measure. Write $\\mu_H$ for its image on $G/H$. If $$\\delta=\\sum_{i=1}^r\n \\|\\mu_{H_i}-U_{G/H_i}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}},$$ there is a measure $0\\le f\\le\\mu$, of mass $z\\ge1-\\delta$, with $$f(gH_i)\\le [G:H_i]^{-1}\\qquad(g\\in G,\\ 1\\le i\\le r).$$ It can be obtained by successive trimming of excess coset mass or by taking the pointwise minimum of the separately trimmed measures. When $z>0$, its normalization is within $1-z$ of $\\mu$ in TV and has coset cap $z^{-1}$ times uniform.*\n\n*Alternatively, delete the union of all cosets whose original $\\mu$-mass exceeds twice uniform. The deleted mass is $\\eta\\le2\\delta$. If $\\delta<1/2$, the normalized retained law is exactly $\\eta$ away from $\\mu$ in TV and has coset cap $2/(1-\\eta)$ times uniform.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-fourier-inputs",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $u>0$, $C_u<\\infty$ and $Z_u(s)\\to0$ as $s\\to\\infty$.*\n\n*Let $H_1,\\ldots,H_r$ be subgroups of a finite group $G$, and let $\\mu$ be a probability measure. Write $\\mu_H$ for its image on $G/H$. If $$\\delta=\\sum_{i=1}^r\n \\|\\mu_{H_i}-U_{G/H_i}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}},$$ there is a measure $0\\le f\\le\\mu$, of mass $z\\ge1-\\delta$, with $$f(gH_i)\\le [G:H_i]^{-1}\\qquad(g\\in G,\\ 1\\le i\\le r).$$ It can be obtained by successive trimming of excess coset mass or by taking the pointwise minimum of the separately trimmed measures. When $z>0$, its normalization is within $1-z$ of $\\mu$ in TV and has coset cap $z^{-1}$ times uniform.*\n\n*Alternatively, delete the union of all cosets whose original $\\mu$-mass exceeds twice uniform. The deleted mass is $\\eta\\le2\\delta$. If $\\delta<1/2$, the normalized retained law is exactly $\\eta$ away from $\\mu$ in TV and has coset cap $2/(1-\\eta)$ times uniform.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-disjoint-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ have $N$ elements and $G=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(X)$. Let $M_1,\\ldots,M_r$ be disjoint nonempty subsets of $X$, where $r\\ge1$, and put $H_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(M_i)$, fixing the complement pointwise. Their union need not be $X$. Suppose $f\\ge0$ has mass $z\\le1$ and $$f(gH_i)\\le K[G:H_i]^{-1}\\qquad(g\\in G,\\ 1\\le i\\le r)$$ for some $K\\ge0$. For an irreducible unitary representation $\\rho$ of degree $D$, set $$S_i(D)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\widehat H_i\\\\ \\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/r}}}\n       (\\dim\\tau)^2.$$ For every $u>0$, the separate orbit and isotypic estimates are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n &\\le \\left(\\frac{zK}{D}\\sum_iS_i(D)\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\sqrt{zKrC_u}\\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2r)},\n \\label{shear:eq:noncovering-orbit-general}\\\\\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n &\\le \\frac{zK}{D}\\sum_iS_i(D)\n \\le zKrC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:noncovering-isotypic-general}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, when $r>4$, the inverse-square orbit estimate gives $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le\\sqrt{rKC_2}\\,D^{-(1-4/r)/2}.\n \\label{shear:eq:orbit-bound}$$ A separate coefficient-projection argument gives $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le\\frac{K^2}{D}\\sum_{i=1}^r\n       \\sum_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\widehat H_i\\\\\n                            \\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/r}}}(\\dim\\tau)^2\n \\le K^2rC_{10}D^{-1+12/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-general}$$ Finally, for $\\rho=\\rho_\\lambda$ with $\\lambda\\vdash N$, put $$k_\\lambda=N-\\max(\\lambda_1,\\lambda'_1),\n \\qquad M(k)=\\sum_{j=0}^k p(j),$$ where $p(j)$ is the partition number and $p(0)=1$. The branching refinement is $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho_\\lambda)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le zKrM(k_\\lambda)D^{-1+2/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-branching}$$ All these statements allow unequal support sizes and unrestricted restriction multiplicities. In particular they include the probability-law cases $z=1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-disjoint-transfer",

For an irreducible unitary representation $\\rho$ of degree $D$, set $$S_i(D)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\widehat H_i\\\\ \\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/r}}}\n       (\\dim\\tau)^2.$$ For every $u>0$, the separate orbit and isotypic estimates are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n &\\le \\left(\\frac{zK}{D}\\sum_iS_i(D)\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\sqrt{zKrC_u}\\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2r)},\n \\label{shear:eq:noncovering-orbit-general}\\\\\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n &\\le \\frac{zK}{D}\\sum_iS_i(D)\n \\le zKrC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:noncovering-isotypic-general}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, when $r>4$, the inverse-square orbit estimate gives $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le\\sqrt{rKC_2}\\,D^{-(1-4/r)/2}.\n \\label{shear:eq:orbit-bound}$$ A separate coefficient-projection argument gives $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le\\frac{K^2}{D}\\sum_{i=1}^r\n       \\sum_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\widehat H_i\\\\\n                            \\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/r}}}(\\dim\\tau)^2\n \\le K^2rC_{10}D^{-1+12/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-general}$$ Finally, for $\\rho=\\rho_\\lambda$ with $\\lambda\\vdash N$, put $$k_\\lambda=N-\\max(\\lambda_1,\\lambda'_1),\n \\qquad M(k)=\\sum_{j=0}^k p(j),$$ where $p(j)$ is the partition number and $p(0)=1$. The branching refinement is $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho_\\lambda)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le zKrM(k_\\lambda)D^{-1+2/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-branching}$$ All these statements allow unequal support sizes and unrestricted restriction multiplicities.       "exact_statement": "Let $X$ have $N$ elements and $G=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(X)$. The branching refinement is $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho_\\lambda)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le zKrM(k_\\lambda)D^{-1+2/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-branching}$$ All these statements allow unequal support sizes and unrestricted restriction multiplicities.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-subprobability-fourier",
        "exact_quote": "For a complex mass function $a$ on a finite group $G$, $$\\sum_\\rho D_\\rho\\|\\widehat a(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n =|G|\\sum_g|a(g)|^2.\n \\label{shear:eq:plancherel-mass}$$ If $\\xi\\ge0$ has mass $m\\le1$, its transform is not divided by $m$, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n |G|\\sum_g\\left|\\xi(g)-\\frac{m}{|G|}\\right|^2\n &=\\sum_{\\rho\\ne\\mathrm{triv}}D_\\rho\n                   \\|\\widehat\\xi(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2,\n \\label{eq:subprobability-centered}\\\\\n \\left(\\sum_g|\\xi(g)-U_G(g)|\\right)^2\n &\\le(1-m)^2+\\sum_{\\rho\\ne\\mathrm{triv}}D_\\rho\n                   \\|\\widehat\\xi(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2.\n \\label{eq:subprobability-full}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $\\xi\\le\\mu$ for a probability measure $\\mu$, then $\\sum_g|\\mu(g)-\\xi(g)|=1-m$. When $m>0$, $\\|\\xi/m-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\le1-m$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-frames-average-marginals",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge6$, $b=64$, $q=N(1-1/b)$, $T=2048d$, and $\\mu_T=q_d^{*T}$. The average over uniform ordered lists of $q$ distinct input labels of the total-variation distance of their outputs from a uniform injection is $o(1)$. There is a subprobability $f\\le\\mu_T$ of mass $1-o(1)$ and a partition into 64 equal parts for which every complementary marginal of $f$ is bounded above by the corresponding uniform marginal.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-matching-invariance",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $T\\ge d$ and directions $v_1,\\ldots,v_T$ such that every consecutive $d$ form a basis. There are invertible linear maps $L_0,\\ldots,L_T$ with $L_0e_i=v_i$ for $1\\le i\\le d$ such that, if $g_t$ is the cyclic-layer process, then $L_tg_tL_0^{-1}$ is the independent switch process with directions $v_t$. This assertion is an equality in law conditional on the entire fixed sequence. If $L_0=I$, the matching on trajectory labels before each switch is unchanged. For general $L_0$, the same statement holds after relabeling the initial labels by $L_0$. Consequently the average, over uniform ordered input lists of any fixed length, of the endpoint distance to a uniform injection is unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },

This assertion is an equality in law conditional on the entire fixed sequence. If $L_0=I$, the matching on trajectory labels before each switch is unchanged. For general $L_0$, the same statement holds after relabeling the initial labels by $L_0$. Consequently the average, over uniform ordered input lists of any fixed length, of the endpoint distance to a uniform injection is unchanged.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-holes",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $T\\ge1$ and nonzero directions $v_1,\\ldots,v_T$. Let $Z_0=I$ and $Z_t=\\xi_tZ_{t-1}$, where the $\\xi_t$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_t$. Fix complete feasible trajectories of some observed labels. If $m\\ge1$ labels remain unobserved, let $F_t$ be the positions not occupied by the observed labels at time $t$. For a specified unobserved label, let $p_t$ be its conditional position law given those complete observed trajectories.*\n\n*The vector $p_t$ evolves from its initial point mass by averaging on pairs with two positions in $F_{t-1}$ and deterministic transport on pairs with one such position. It depends only on the directions and observed paths through time $t$. The uniform vector $1/m$ is transported to the uniform vector on $F_t$. Thus, with $E_t=\\sum_{x\\in F_t}(p_t(x)-1/m)^2$, one has $0\\le E_t\\le1$ and $$E_{t-1}-E_t=\\frac12\\sum_{\\{x,y\\}\\subset F_{t-1}\\text{ paired at }t}\n                    (p_{t-1}(x)-p_{t-1}(y))^2.\n \\label{frames:hole-loss}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-holes",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $T\\ge1$ and nonzero directions $v_1,\\ldots,v_T$. Let $Z_0=I$ and $Z_t=\\xi_tZ_{t-1}$, where the $\\xi_t$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_t$. Fix complete feasible trajectories of some observed labels. If $m\\ge1$ labels remain unobserved, let $F_t$ be the positions not occupied by the observed labels at time $t$. For a specified unobserved label, let $p_t$ be its conditional position law given those complete observed trajectories.*\n\n*The vector $p_t$ evolves from its initial point mass by averaging on pairs with two positions in $F_{t-1}$ and deterministic transport on pairs with one such position. It depends only on the directions and observed paths through time $t$. The uniform vector $1/m$ is transported to the uniform vector on $F_t$. Thus, with $E_t=\\sum_{x\\in F_t}(p_t(x)-1/m)^2$, one has $0\\le E_t\\le1$ and $$E_{t-1}-E_t=\\frac12\\sum_{\\{x,y\\}\\subset F_{t-1}\\text{ paired at }t}\n                    (p_{t-1}(x)-p_{t-1}(y))^2.\n \\label{frames:hole-loss}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-conditional-interface",
        "exact_quote": "Fix integers $T\\ge1$ and $1\\le q\\le n$. Let $\\mathbf v=(v_1,\\ldots,v_T)$ be a random schedule of nonzero directions. Set $Z_0=I$, and suppose that, conditional on $\\mathbf v$, the increments of $Z$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_1,\\ldots,v_T$. Let $C=(c_1,\\ldots,c_q)$ be a deterministic list of distinct labels, or a random such list independent of the joint process $(\\mathbf v,(Z_t)_{t=0}^T)$. For $1\\le j\\le q$, put $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal F_t^{C,j}\n &=\\sigma\\bigl(C,v_1,\\ldots,v_t,\\,\n       Z_s(c_i):0\\le s\\le t,\\ i<j\\bigr), \\label{frames:online-field}\\\\\n \\mathcal H^{C,j}\n &=\\sigma\\bigl(C,\\mathbf v,\\,\n       Z_s(c_i):0\\le s\\le T,\\ i<j\\bigr). \\label{frames:terminal-field}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $0\\le t\\le T$, define $p_t(x)=\\Pr\\{Z_t(c_j)=x\\mid\\mathcal F_t^{C,j}\\}$. Then, for every $x\\in V$, $$\\Pr\\{Z_t(c_j)=x\\mid\\mathcal H^{C,j}\\}=p_t(x)\n \\quad\\text{almost surely}. \\label{frames:full-path-prefix}$$ For $t<T$, let $K_t(v)=\\Pr\\{v_{t+1}=v\\mid v_1,\\ldots,v_t\\}$. Then $$\\Pr\\{Z_t(c_j)=x,\\ v_{t+1}=v\\mid\\mathcal F_t^{C,j}\\}\n =p_t(x)K_t(v). \\label{frames:direction-factorization}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-cross-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose, given the generated past, the next direction is uniform outside a hyperplane $J\\subset V$. Split the $m$ unobserved positions into the two cosets, denoting the resulting sets by $F_0,F_1$ and their sizes by $m_0,m_1$. For any centered real mass vector $z$ known from that past, the expected squared-norm loss obtained by averaging on the paired unobserved positions is $$\\frac1n\\sum_{x\\in F_0,\\ y\\in F_1}(z_x-z_y)^2\n \\ge \\frac{\\min(m_0,m_1)}n\\sum_xz_x^2.$$ If the next direction is uniform outside a smaller subspace contained in $J$, the same lower bound holds with $1/(2n)$ in place of $1/n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-cross-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose, given the generated past, the next direction is uniform outside a hyperplane $J\\subset V$.           "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
```
