    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-crossing-balance",
        "exact_quote": "Fix integers $T\\ge d$ and $r\\ge1$. For any fixed observed labels and any $d<t\\le T$, if $m$ positions remain unobserved, then $$\\Pr\\{\\min(|F_{t-1}\\cap J_t|,|F_{t-1}\\setminus J_t|)<m/4\\}\n \\le2e^{-m/8}.$$ For the hidden-card energy of Lemma <a href=\"#frames:holes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:holes\">38</a>, interpreted in this random process by Corollary <a href=\"#frames:conditional-interface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:conditional-interface\">39</a>, if $m\\ge n/r$ and $\\alpha=1/(4r)$, then $$\\mathbb E E_T\\le(1-\\alpha)^{T-d}+2e^{-n/(8r)}.\n \\label{frames:crossing-recursion}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-crossing-balance",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-backward-removal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T\\ge d$ be an integer, and let $r\\ge2$ divide $n$. Partition $V$ into equal input blocks $M_1,\\ldots,M_r$ and, for each $i$, fix an order $c_{i,1},\\ldots,c_{i,q}$ on its complement, where $q=n-n/r$. Write $\\mathcal P$ for the partition together with these orders, and let $K_i=\\operatorname{Sym}(M_i)$ fix the complement pointwise. Use the variable-direction process just defined, whose first frame is $L_0=I$. For $1\\le j\\le q$, let $$\\mathcal H_{i,j}\n =\\sigma\\bigl(\\mathbf v,\\,\n       Z_s(c_{i,h}):0\\le s\\le T,\\ h<j\\bigr),\\qquad\n F_{i,j}=V\\setminus\\{Z_T(c_{i,h}):h<j\\},$$ and define $$p_{i,j}(x)=\\Pr\\{Z_T(c_{i,j})=x\\mid\\mathcal H_{i,j}\\},\\qquad\n E_{i,j}=\\sum_{x\\in F_{i,j}}\n       \\left(p_{i,j}(x)-\\frac1{n-j+1}\\right)^2.$$ Suppose $\\mathbb E E_{i,j}\\le\\varepsilon_n$ uniformly. Put $\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P}=\\bigcap_{i,j}\\{E_{i,j}\\le n^{-6}\\}$, and let $\\mu$ be the original cyclic endpoint law at time $T$. Then $\\Pr(\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P}^c)\\le rq n^6\\varepsilon_n$. The measure $$\\nu(g)=\\Pr\\{\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P},\\ L_T^{-1}Z_T=g\\}$$ satisfies $0\\le\\nu\\le\\mu$ and, for large $n$, $\\nu(gK_i)\\le2U_G(gK_i)$ for every $g\\in G$ and $1\\le i\\le r$. If $a=\\Pr(\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P})=1-o(1)$, its normalized law $\\bar\\nu=\\nu/a$ satisfies $\\bar\\nu(gK_i)\\le4U_G(gK_i)$ and $\\|\\bar\\nu-\\mu\\|_{\\rm TV}\\le1-a=o(1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-backward-removal",

Use the variable-direction process just defined, whose first frame is $L_0=I$. Put $\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P}=\\bigcap_{i,j}\\{E_{i,j}\\le n^{-6}\\}$, and let $\\mu$ be the original cyclic endpoint law at time $T$. The measure $$\\nu(g)=\\Pr\\{\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P},\\ L_T^{-1}Z_T=g\\}$$ satisfies $0\\le\\nu\\le\\mu$ and, for large $n$, $\\nu(gK_i)\\le2U_G(gK_i)$ for every $g\\in G$ and $1\\le i\\le r$. If $a=\\Pr(\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P})=1-o(1)$, its normalized law $\\bar\\nu=\\nu/a$ satisfies $\\bar\\nu(gK_i)\\le4U_G(gK_i)$ and $\\|\\bar\\nu-\\mu\\|_{\\rm TV}\\le1-a=o(1)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-frames-stopped-marginals",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge4$. Put $b=16$, $m=n/b$, $q=n-m$, and $T=(2+20b)d=322d$.         "anchor": "stmt-frames-reference-rows",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a realized cyclic history $g=(g_0,\\ldots,g_T)$ and a nonempty set $F$ of labels. For $u\\in F$, let $p_t^{u,F}(x)$ be the conditional probability under $\\mathbb P$ that card $u$ is at $x$ at time $t$, given the complete trajectories of the labels in $V\\setminus F$, evaluated on the trajectories supplied by $g$. Define a row indexed by $F$ by $$r_t^{u,F}(v)=p_t^{u,F}(g_t(v)),\\qquad v\\in F.$$ Then, for this realized history, $$r_0^{u,F}=e_u^{\\mathsf T},\\qquad\n r_t^{u,F}=r_{t-1}^{u,F}B_t^F,\n \\qquad r_t^{u,F}=e_u^{\\mathsf T}Q_t^F.\n \\label{frames:reference-row-flow}$$ Here $e_u$ is the coordinate vector in $\\mathbb R^F$. In particular the conditional likelihood of the realized endpoint of card $u$ is $$p_T^{u,F}(g_T(u))=Q_T^F(u,u).\n \\label{frames:diagonal-shadow}$$ The conditioning in this statement remains on the paths of $V\\setminus F$; the realized history enters $r_t^{u,F}$ through the indexing bijection $v\\mapsto g_t(v)$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-aux-directions",
        "exact_quote": "Conditional on $\\Lambda$, the increments of $X$ are independent uniform switches in the coordinate directions $e_{\\iota_i}$. For every $t\\le T$, the conditional law of $(Y_i)_{i=0}^t$ given $\\Lambda$ is the product-switch law determined by $w_1,\\ldots,w_t$.*\n\n*Every $d$ consecutive directions $w_i$ form a basis. At layer $i$, learning $w_i$ does not change the conditional law of the preceding virtual positions given $w_1,\\ldots,w_{i-1}$ and any specified virtual path prefixes through layer $i-1$. For $i>d$, that direction is uniform outside $$\\operatorname{span}(w_{i-d+1},\\ldots,w_{i-1})$$ conditional on that same direction and path-prefix field. Finally, conditional on the complete direction sequence $W$, disclosing the remaining information in $\\Lambda$ does not change the conditional law of the virtual trajectories, including after a fixed collection of those trajectories has been observed.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-aux-directions",

At layer $i$, learning $w_i$ does not change the conditional law of the preceding virtual positions given $w_1,\\ldots,w_{i-1}$ and any specified virtual path prefixes through layer $i-1$. For $i>d$, that direction is uniform outside $$\\operatorname{span}(w_{i-d+1},\\ldots,w_{i-1})$$ conditional on that same direction and path-prefix field. Finally, conditional on the complete direction sequence $W$, disclosing the remaining information in $\\Lambda$ does not change the conditional law of the virtual trajectories, including after a fixed collection of those trajectories has been observed.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-aux-marginal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C=(a_1,\\ldots,a_k)$ be a uniformly chosen ordered list of distinct initial cards, independent of $X_T\\sim\\mu$, and let $\\pi_k$ be uniform on ordered $k$-tuples of distinct positions. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_C\\bigl\\|\\mathcal L(X_T(C)\\mid C)-\\pi_k\\bigr\\|_{\\rm TV}\n &\\le\\varepsilon_n,\\notag\\\\\n \\varepsilon_n\n &=\\frac{k}{2}\\sqrt{n\\bigl(e^{-25d}+T\\delta_n\\bigr)}=o(1).\n \\label{frames:aux:marginal-error}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-238.2"

      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $C=(a_1,\\ldots,a_k)$ be a uniformly chosen ordered list of distinct initial cards, independent of $X_T\\sim\\mu$, and let $\\pi_k$ be uniform on ordered $k$-tuples of distinct positions.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-aux-orbit-lift",
        "exact_quote": "If $\\rho$ is an irreducible unitary representation of $G=S_n$ of dimension $D$, then the measure in <a href=\"#frames:aux:cosets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"frames:aux:cosets\">[frames:aux:cosets]</a> satisfies $$\\label{frames:aux:operator-bound}\n \\|\\widehat\\nu(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le C_rD^{-1/2+11/r},\\qquad C_r=\\sqrt{2rK_0}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-aux-orbit-lift",
          "exact_quote": "If $\\rho$ is an irreducible unitary representation of $G=S_n$ of dimension $D$, then the measure in <a href=\"#frames:aux:cosets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"frames:aux:cosets\">[frames:aux:cosets]</a> satisfies $$\\label{frames:aux:operator-bound}\n \\|\\widehat\\nu(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le C_rD^{-1/2+11/r},\\qquad C_r=\\sqrt{2rK_0}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $\\rho$ is an irreducible unitary representation of $G=S_n$ of dimension $D$, then the measure in <a href=\"#frames:aux:cosets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"frames:aux:cosets\">[frames:aux:cosets]</a> satisfies $$\\label{frames:aux:operator-bound}\n \\|\\widehat\\nu(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le C_rD^{-1/2+11/r},\\qquad C_r=\\sqrt{2rK_0}.$$",
      "explanation": "The common trimmed coset law contracts every irreducible Fourier matrix through an inverse-degree orbit estimate.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-aux-orbit-lift",
          "exact_quote": "If $\\rho$ is an irreducible unitary representation of $G=S_n$ of dimension $D$,",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "frames:aux:cosets",
          "exact_quote": "Two position permutations agree on the complement of $C_i$ exactly when they lie in the same left coset $gH_i$. Thus the corresponding coset marginal of $\\mu$ is within its complement marginal distance of uniform on $G/H_i$, where $G=S_n$. Delete from $\\mu$ every element lying in a coset, for any $i$, whose $\\mu$-mass exceeds $2/[G:H_i]$, and call the resulting subprobability measure $\\nu$. Writing $p=\\nu(G)$, we obtain $$\\label{frames:aux:cosets}\n 1-p\\le2r\\varepsilon_n=o(1),\\qquad\n \\nu(gH_i)\\le\\frac2{[G:H_i]}\\quad(g\\in G,\\ 1\\le i\\le r).$$ Indeed, if a coset has mass $a>2u$, where $u=1/[G:H_i]$, then $a\\le2(a-u)$. Its removed mass is bounded by twice its positive excess over uniform; summing positive excesses gives exactly the marginal total-variation distance. A union bound handles all $r$ families of deleted cosets.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-auxiliary-orbit-decay",
      "kind": "bound",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-frames-aux-orbit-lift"
    },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Physical Thorp shuffle** ([source](#eq:physical-step)): In one *physical shuffle*, the deck is split into its top and bottom halves, and, for $i=0,\ldots,n/2-1$, the cards at index $i$ within the two halves occupy output positions $2i$ and $2i+1$ in an independently chosen fair order.
- **Label-to-position state** ([source](#sec:prelim)): A state $g\in G$ maps a label to its current position, and permutations are composed as functions: $(gh)(x)=g(h(x))$.
- **Total-variation distance** ([source](#eq:worst-state)): For probability measures on a finite set, we use $$\\|\mu-\nu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} =\frac12\sum_x\|\mu(x)-\nu(x)\| =\max_A\|\mu(A)-\nu(A)\|.$$
- **Mixing time** ([source](#eq:tmix)): $$\label{eq:tmix} t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)= \min\{t\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}:\\|q_d^{*t}-U\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le1/4\}.$$ Total variation is nonincreasing in $t$ by contraction under convolution. The minimum exists for every $d\ge1$; the coordinate description in Section <a href="#sec:frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:frames">3</a> gives a short proof. Our estimates below give the quantitative bound as $d$ grows.
- **Directional matching subgroup** ([source](#sec:frames)): For a nonzero vector $v\in V$, let $C_v\le G$ be the subgroup of permutations that preserve each pair $\{x,x+v\}$ setwise.
- **Rotating-frame coordinate process** ([source](#eq:frames:cyclic)): In the rotating position frame, put $$\label{eq:frames:cyclic} X_t=R^{-t}Y_t,\qquad Q_t=R^{-(t-1)}S_tR^{t-1}.$$
- **Chronological hidden-card observation field** ([source](#eq:marginal-filtration)): For its $j$th label define the exact online field $$\label{eq:marginal-filtration} \mathcal F_t^{j} =\sigma\bigl(\Xi,v_1,\ldots,v_t,\, Z_s(c_i):0\le s\le t,\ i<j\bigr).$$
- **Hidden-card energy** ([source](#eq:marginal-energy)): $$\label{eq:marginal-energy} p_t^j(x)=\Pr\{Z_t(c_j)=x\mid\mathcal F_t^j\},\qquad \Delta_t^j=\sum_{x\in D_t^j}\left(p_t^j(x)-\frac1{r_j}\right)^2.$$ We extend $p_t^j$ by zero outside $D_t^j$. It is a probability vector on that set, so $0\le\Delta_t^j=\sum_xp_t^j(x)^2-1/r_j\le1$.
- **Coset pushforward** ([source](#sec:lift)): If $\mu$ is a probability measure on $G$, write $\mu_H$ for its pushforward to the set $G/H$ of left cosets.
- **Finite-group Fourier matrix** ([source](#sec:lift)): For a mass function $a:G\to\mathbb C$ and a unitary representation $\rho$, define its Fourier matrix by $$\widehat a(\rho)=\sum_{g\in G}a(g)\rho(g).$$
- **Tableau representation degree** ([source](#sec:degrees)): Recall that the complex irreducible representations of $S_s$ are indexed by partitions $\lambda\vdash s$, and that their degrees $f^\lambda$ count standard Young tableaux of shape $\lambda$.
- **Typical entropy history event** ([source](#stmt-frames-typical-history)): Define the event on cyclic histories by $$\label{frames:typical-average} \mathcal G_{\mathrm{ent}} =\bigcap_{m=n/L}^{n} \left\{g:\mathbb E_{F:\,\|F\|=m}Z_T^F(g)\le n^{-8}\right\},$$ where each average is uniform over subsets of labels.
- **Quotient density cutoff kernel** ([source](#lifts:kernel-cutoff)): For $\epsilon>0$, retain exactly the quotient blocks below the specified density threshold: $$T_i(g,z)=K(g,z)\, \mathbf1\left\{p_i(gH_i,zH_i)\le\frac{1+\epsilon}{L}\right\}. \label{lifts:kernel-cutoff}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-support-distance | Full-permutation total-variation distance after physical shuffles | ≥ | $1-\frac{2^{tn/2}}{n!}$ | Every nonnegative integer physical horizon. |
| q-sharp-support-time | Mixing time at the specified total-variation threshold | ≥ | $\left\lceil\frac2n\log_2\left(\frac{3n!}{4}\right)\right\rceil$ | Dyadic deck dimensions; the asymptotic interpretation is given in the statement. |
| q-one-card-obstruction | Full-permutation total-variation distance inferred from a card location | ≥ | $1-2^t/n$ | Before a full coordinate sweep. |
| q-trimmed-coset-cap | Conditional probability of a subgroup coset | ≤ | $\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{\|H_a\|}{\|G\|}$ | The removed mass and coset systems of the trimming lemma. |
| q-coset-operator-decay | Fourier operator norm under simultaneous coset caps | ≤ | $\sqrt{kBC_0}\,D^{-(1-18/k)/2}$ | Disjoint nonempty partition blocks with the stated lower bound on their count. |
| q-fixed-list-error | Fixed ordered input-list distance from a uniform injection | ≤ | $\frac{N^{3/2}}2 \left(e^{-(T-d)/(4L)}+2e^{-N/(8L)}\right)^{1/2}.$$$ | The omitted fraction is fixed and the estimate is uniform over lists and axis orders. |
| q-fixed-list-energy-at-first-transfer | Energy bound used for the fixed-list transfer | = | $(31/32)^{255d}+64e^{-N/64}$ | The listed omitted fraction and physical horizon. |
| q-inverse-degree-constant | Uniform complete inverse-degree sum | = | $\sup_{s\ge 1}\sum_{\lambda\vdash s}(f^\lambda)^{-16}$ | All positive integer group sizes. |
| q-strongest-mixing-upper | Eventual upper bound on physical mixing time | ≤ | $512d$ | Asymptotically large dyadic deck dimension at the specified mixing threshold. |
| q-stopped-tuple-error | Stopped fixed-list distance from a uniform injection | ≤ | $\frac{4T}{m}+\frac12n^{3/2} (1-1/(4b))^{(T-2d+1)/2}$ | The stopped argument's stated reservoir and horizon. |
| q-half-sweep-trace | Normalized squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm of a sweep transform | ≤ | $2D_\alpha^{-1/100}$ | Every irreducible type in the eventual large-set regime. |
| q-half-marginal-repair-cost | Total-variation cost of repairing the permutation law | ≤ | $\epsilon_1+\epsilon_2+3\epsilon_{\mathrm{part}}$ | The original half-injection errors and output-partition error. |

        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-support-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lower): Count switch outcomes and compare their support with uniform mass. -> Support and mixing-time lower bounds.
- **step-single-card** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-card-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-card-lower): Bound successor support and refresh each coordinate over a sweep. -> Single-card lower obstruction and endpoint uniformity.
- **step-frame-construction** [claim](#stmt-shear-lem-general-frame) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-lem-general-frame): Construct invertible column updates and telescope transformed switches. -> Conditional complete path identity.
- **step-frame-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-cor-frames-marginals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-frames-marginals): Push tuples through terminal bijections and average the initial input map. -> Same-list or averaged-list distance identity.
- **step-online-energy** [claim](#stmt-shear-lem-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-lem-energy): Separate averaging edges from transport edges and average fresh cross differences. -> Exact decrement and conditional contraction.
- **step-terminal-tuple** [claim](#stmt-shear-lem-tuple) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-lem-tuple): Coarsen to predecessor endpoints and use sequential variation comparison. -> Ordinary tuple total-variation bound.
- **step-select-partition** [claim](#stmt-prop-marginals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-marginals): Sample an omitted block, iterate the unconditional energy recurrence, and select a partition attaining its average. -> Deterministic partition with small summed errors.
- **step-simultaneous-trimming** [claim](#stmt-lem-trim) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trim): Delete the union of heavy cosets and normalize the retained mass. -> One nearby probability with simultaneous density caps.
- **step-single-vector-orbits** [claim](#stmt-prop-lift-orbit-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lift-orbit-sum): Assign product types to small factors and Schur-average single-vector orbit projections. -> Fourier operator bound by squared subgroup degrees.
- **step-tableau-sums** [claim](#stmt-lem-degrees) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degrees): Separate diagrams by width and height and count extendible tableaux. -> Uniform inverse-degree summability and nonlinear vanishing.
- **step-local-fourier-decay** [claim](#stmt-prop-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lift): Insert inverse-degree summability into the orbit degree sum. -> Polynomial Fourier operator decay.
- **step-plancherel** [claim](#stmt-lem-plancherel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-plancherel): Apply regular-character orthogonality and Cauchy–Schwarz. -> Full-group variation bound.
- **step-parity** [claim](#stmt-lem-fair-sign) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fair-sign): Toggle an independent final switch and bound perturbations against sign. -> Exact parity cancellation and perturbation bounds.
- **step-standalone-completion** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fair-sign-2): Convolve independently trimmed blocks, sum nonlinear Fourier contributions, and restore the true factors. -> Standalone worst-start mixing theorem.
- **step-concentration** [claim](#stmt-lem-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-concentration): Bound exponential moments and expose uniform-subset draws. -> Independent, martingale, and half-count tail estimates.
- **step-fixed-balance** [claim](#stmt-shear-lem-fixed-balance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-lem-fixed-balance): Use the previous transverse layer and preservation by intervening directions. -> Unconditional fixed-list balance probability.
- **step-fixed-marginals** [claim](#stmt-shear-prop-fixed-marginal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-prop-fixed-marginal): Iterate balanced energy contraction and transfer forward and reversed layers through terminal frames. -> Uniform forward and inverse fixed-list error bounds.
- **step-shear-realization** [claim](#stmt-shear-lem-keys) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-lem-keys): Read independent shear entries when their target column is next selected. -> Fresh virtual directions and conditional prefix laws.
- **step-reservoir-balance** [claim](#stmt-shear-prop-reservoir) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-prop-reservoir): Condition on shuffle history before sampling the omitted reservoir and bound half-count imbalance. -> Average complementary tuple error.
- **step-averaged-tuples** [claim](#stmt-lifts-averaged-tuples) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lifts-averaged-tuples): Use uniform available subsets after averaging input labels. -> Average conditional tuple-distance bound.

- **step-ordinary-average-law** [claim](#stmt-frames-average-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-average-law): Combine subset moments with proper-subspace energy loss. -> Nearly complete ordinary random-input marginals.

- **step-companion-inputs** [claim](#stmt-shear-prop-fourier-inputs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-prop-fourier-inputs): Import all-positive-power degree summability and common trimming results. -> Retained-law caps and normalized comparison costs.
- **step-disjoint-transfers** [claim](#stmt-shear-prop-disjoint-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-prop-disjoint-transfer): Use a common small-type cover for orbit assignment, central projections, coefficient norms, and branching counts. -> Separate operator and Hilbert–Schmidt estimates.
- **step-mass-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-subprobability-fourier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subprobability-fourier): Apply complex Plancherel after centering by the retained mass. -> Missing-mass and normalization inequalities.
- **step-isotypic-fast-mixing** [claim](#stmt-shear-cor-512) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-cor-512): Trim complementary marginals exactly and amplify the companion isotypic estimate. -> The strongest stated explicit full-deck horizon.
- **step-orbit-applications** [claim](#stmt-shear-thm-2048) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-thm-2048): Apply orbit decay to normalized deletion or retained subprobabilities and restore true block laws. -> Orbit-based physical mixing horizons.
- **step-average-retained-amplification** [claim](#stmt-frames-average-marginals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-average-marginals): Select a partition from averaged list errors and amplify its dominated retained law. -> Simultaneous marginal domination and full-deck convergence.
- **step-coefficient-amplification** [claim](#stmt-shear-thm-32-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-thm-32-block): Normalize exact trimming and convolve the coefficient-projection bound. -> Separate coefficient-based physical mixing horizon.
- **step-branching-amplification** [claim](#stmt-shear-thm-4096) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-thm-4096): Absorb branching type counts into ambient representation degree. -> Branching-based physical mixing horizon.
- **step-optimal-order** [claim](#stmt-shear-cor-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shear-cor-order): Combine the strongest upper horizon with support and single-card obstructions. -> Optimal mixing order.
- **step-matching-invariance** [claim](#stmt-frames-matching-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-matching-invariance): Use selected-column identities to preserve matching trajectory labels. -> Matching-functional invariance and input-average preservation.
- **step-complete-path-flow** [claim](#stmt-frames-holes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-holes): Identify the observed-path event as a cylinder in the independent coin array. -> Prefix-determined hidden mass despite full-path conditioning.
- **step-conditioning-fields** [claim](#stmt-frames-conditional-interface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-conditional-interface): Apply the tower property to complete and chronological fields. -> Hidden-position and next-direction factorization.
- **step-centered-contraction** [claim](#stmt-frames-cross-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-cross-energy): Apply the bipartite centered-vector identity to the matching loss. -> Cross-hyperplane squared-norm contraction.
- **step-crossing-recurrence** [claim](#stmt-frames-crossing-balance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-crossing-balance): Use fixed-schedule transverse balance and then average only in the chronological field. -> Expected terminal energy bound.

- **step-retained-event-applications** [claim](#stmt-frames-pointwise-applications) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-pointwise-applications): Choose partition parameters, bound exceptional mass, and amplify orbit contraction. -> Separate retained-event physical mixing horizons.
- **step-stopped-comparison** [claim](#stmt-frames-stopped-marginals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-stopped-marginals): Nest balance events, bound their smallest reservoir once, and couple prescribed endpoint kernels sequentially. -> Uniform fixed-list stopped error bound.
- **step-pruning-application** [claim](#stmt-frames-pruning-application) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-pruning-application): Apply companion spectral pruning to the stopped complementary marginals. -> Full-deck pruning horizon.

- **step-typical-history** [claim](#stmt-frames-typical-history) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-typical-history): Transfer matching functionals to fresh directions, average reservoirs, and bound exceptional subset-average energies. -> One high-probability event for all required reservoir sizes.

- **step-entropy-tail** [claim](#stmt-frames-entropy-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-entropy-tail): Apply the companion all-tilt coefficient theorem and split absolute coefficients at its threshold. -> Coefficient tail and conditional endpoint operator bounds.
- **step-entropy-amplification** [claim](#stmt-frames-entropy-application) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-entropy-application): Convolve conditional typical histories, sum degree contributions, and remove their conditioning. -> Typical-history full-deck mixing application.

- **step-auxiliary-energy** [claim](#stmt-frames-aux-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-aux-energy): Average a random input ordering and union-bound affine half-space imbalance. -> Uniform auxiliary expected energy bound.
- **step-auxiliary-marginal** [claim](#stmt-frames-aux-marginal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-aux-marginal): Disclose terminal shear data and push virtual endpoints through the known output map. -> Averaged nearly complete tuple error.

- **step-auxiliary-completion** [claim](#stmt-frames-aux-theorem) / [proof](#pm-h-43): Center retained convolution powers, separate parity, and restore missing mass and physical coordinates. -> Auxiliary worst-start mixing theorem.
- **step-random-four-blocks** [claim](#stmt-lifts-random-four-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lifts-random-four-blocks): Select a partition from averaged inputs, trim commonly, and amplify the isotypic norm. -> Random-input full-deck mixing application.
- **step-sixteen-kernels** [claim](#stmt-lifts-sixteen-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lifts-sixteen-theorem): Retain equivariant quotient blocks and cancel sign-fiber coefficients when ignored positions contain a first-layer pair. -> Uniform retained-kernel full-deck convergence.
- **step-omitted-set-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lifts-neumann-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lifts-neumann-theorem): Keep all omitted sets, apply the companion ordered projection expansion, and convolve the nearby law. -> Overlapping-omitted-set full-deck horizon.
- **step-half-list** [claim](#stmt-frames-char-half-list) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-char-half-list): Specialize fixed-list bounds to halves and include reversed coordinate order. -> Half-image and half-preimage uniformity over phases.
- **step-pass-trace** [claim](#stmt-frames-char-one-pass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-char-one-pass): Count unique prescribed routes, bound fixed-point tails, and remove fixed points in representation restrictions. -> Normalized pass trace decay.
- **step-alternative-sweep-trace** [claim](#stmt-char-sweep-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-char-sweep-trace): Count branching constituents and apply concavity to their character bounds. -> Alternative sweep trace exponent.
- **step-minimum-degree** [claim](#stmt-frames-char-minimum-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-char-minimum-degree): Restrict to matching transpositions and permute their constituent characters. -> Nonlinear minimum dimension bound.
- **step-capped-character-block** [claim](#stmt-frames-char-block-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-char-block-norm): Cap separate half-preimage marginals, compare positive forms, and split large and small subgroup types. -> Uniform modified-block Fourier contraction and zero sign.
- **step-capped-character-completion** [claim](#stmt-frames-char-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-char-block-norm-2): Multiply independently modified block matrices, restore original segments, and undo the terminal rotation. -> Explicit capped-preimage worst-start horizon.
- **step-half-marginal-repair** [claim](#stmt-char-repair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-char-repair): Couple each conditional bijection marginal to uniform and then repair the output partition. -> Exact conditional uniform marginals with a bounded change of law.
- **step-repaired-character-completion** [claim](#stmt-char-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-char-repair-2): Schur-average one uniform subgroup marginal at a time, bound type weights, and amplify independent repaired blocks. -> Symbolic absolute-constant worst-start physical mixing theorem.

          "exact_quote": "Let a finite set $X$ be partitioned into $k>18$ nonempty sets $M_1,\\ldots,M_k$, and put $G=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(X)$. Let $H_a$ be the subgroup that permutes $M_a$ and fixes $X\\setminus M_a$ pointwise. Suppose that a probability measure $\\nu$ on $G$ satisfies, for some $B\\ge1$, $$\\nu(gH_a)\\le B\\frac{|H_a|}{|G|}\n \\qquad(g\\in G,\\ 1\\le a\\le k).$$ Then every irreducible complex unitary representation $\\rho$ of $G$, of dimension $D$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:lift:general-fourier}\n \\|\\widehat\\nu(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le\\sqrt{kBC_0}\\,D^{-(1-18/k)/2}.$$ Here $C_0$ is the absolute constant of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:degrees\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:degrees\">12</a>. The block sizes need not be equal.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-lift"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-concentration",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-concentration",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X_1,\\ldots,X_r$ be independent random variables in $[0,1]$ and put $S=\\sum_iX_i$. For $a\\ge0$ and $r\\ge1$, $$\\Pr(S-\\mathbb ES\\ge a),\\quad\\Pr(S-\\mathbb ES\\le-a)\\le e^{-2a^2/r}.$$ More generally, if $(M_j)_{j=0}^r$ is a real martingale whose increments have absolute value at most $c_j$, either tail at distance $a$ is at most $\\exp[-a^2/(2\\sum_jc_j^2)]$ when the denominator is positive. If all $c_j$ vanish, the martingale is constant. In particular, if $X$ counts the points of a uniform $m$-subset in a fixed half of a finite set of even size, then $$\\Pr(X<m/4)\\le e^{-m/32}\\qquad(m\\ge1).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],

      ],
      "method": "Bound exponential moments and expose uniform-subset draws.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-concentration"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-fixed-marginal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L\\ge2$ be a fixed integer dividing $N$, and let $0\\le q\\le N-N/L$. For every ordered list $C$ of $q$ distinct labels, every periodic ordering $\\boldsymbol\\iota$ of the standard axes, and every integer $T\\ge d$, $$\\label{shear:eq:general-fixed-marginal}\n \\|\\mathcal L(X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_T(C))-\\pi_q\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\le\\frac{N^{3/2}}2\n \\left(e^{-(T-d)/(4L)}+2e^{-N/(8L)}\\right)^{1/2}.$$ The same bound holds for $(X^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_T)^{-1}(C)$. For the physical shuffle and its inverse, the corresponding position laws satisfy the same bound.*\n\n\n\n\n*The following choices used below give distance at most $N^{3/2}\\sqrt{a_T}/2$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n L=8,\\quad T=256d:&\\quad\n a_T=(31/32)^{255d}+64e^{-N/64},\n \\label{shear:eq:256-marginal}\\\\\n L=8,\\quad T=257d:&\\quad\n a_T=e^{-8d}+2e^{-N/64},\n \\label{shear:eq:257-marginal}\\\\\n L=32,\\quad T=2049d:&\\quad\n a_T=e^{-16d}+256e^{-N/256}.\n \\label{shear:eq:2049-marginal}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Each of these three substituted bounds tends to zero as $d\\to\\infty$, uniformly in the list and axis order.           "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-keys",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an axis order $\\boldsymbol\\iota$ and a horizon $T\\ge d$. Independently of the increments in <a href=\"#shear:eq:coordinate-chain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"shear:eq:coordinate-chain\">[shear:eq:coordinate-chain]</a>, choose independent fair bits $b_{sj}$ for $j\\ne i(s)$, put $b_{s,i(s)}=0$, and write $\\Lambda$ for all these entries. Set $$S_s=I+e_{i(s)}b_s,\\qquad\n A_0=I,\\quad A_s=A_{s-1}S_s,\\qquad\n v_s=A_{s-1}e_{i(s)},\\qquad\n Z_s=A_sX^{\\boldsymbol\\iota}_s.$$ Conditional on $\\Lambda$, the increments of $Z$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_s$. More precisely, if $\\mathsf P_{v_1,\\ldots,v_t}$ is the product-switch path law through time $t$ starting at the identity, then $$\\label{shear:eq:prefix-factorization}\n \\mathcal L((Z_s)_{s=0}^t\\mid\\Lambda)\n =\\mathsf P_{v_1,\\ldots,v_t}\\qquad(0\\le t\\le T).$$ Every $d$ consecutive directions form a basis. For $s>d$, conditional on $v_1,\\ldots,v_{s-1}$, the next direction is uniform on $V\\setminus W_s$, with $W_s$ as in <a href=\"#shear:eq:hyperplane\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"shear:eq:hyperplane\">[shear:eq:hyperplane]</a>. This law is unchanged by additionally observing any part of the virtual path prefix through time $s-1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }

Then $$\\label{lifts:averaged-bound}\n \\mathbb E_C\\|\\mu_C-\\pi_{N-m}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\le\\frac{N^{3/2}}2\n \\left((1-1/(4b))^{t-d}+2\\vartheta^{N/b}\\right)^{1/2},\n \\qquad \\vartheta=\\frac32\\,2^{-3/4}<1.$$ The same bound holds for a fixed ordered list of $N-m$ labels when its conditional distance is averaged over a uniformly chosen starting deck.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-delayed-symmetry",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shear-lem-delayed-symmetry",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V=\\mathbb F_2^d$, $N=2^d$, and fix a coordinate $i$. Let $j_1,\\ldots,j_{d-1}$ list all coordinates other than $i$, in any order. Condition on a sigma field $\\mathcal A$ which specifies a nonempty set $B_0\\subseteq V$ of size $h$ and a probability vector $p_0$ supported on $B_0$. Independently of $\\mathcal A$, let $S_r$ be independent uniform matching switches in direction $e_{j_r}$, for $1\\le r\\le d-1$. Define $B_r=S_rB_{r-1}$. On each matching edge, obtain $p_r$ by averaging the two masses when both endpoints belong to $B_{r-1}$, and by moving the mass with its unique available endpoint when exactly one endpoint belongs to $B_{r-1}$; it is zero on edges with no available endpoint. Put $$a_r(x)=p_r(x)-h^{-1}\\mathbf1_{B_r}(x),\\qquad\n E_r=\\sum_{x\\in V}a_r(x)^2,\n \\qquad H_b=\\{x\\in V:x_i=b\\}\\quad(b=0,1).$$ For each $z\\in V$ with $z_i=0$, let $T_zx=x+x_i z$. Conditional on $\\mathcal A$, the terminal pair $(B_{d-1},a_{d-1})$ has the same law as $$\\bigl(T_zB_{d-1},\\ a_{d-1}\\circ T_z^{-1}\\bigr).\n \\label{shear:eq:delayed-symmetry-invariance}$$ The counts $h_b=|B_r\\cap H_b|$ are independent of $r$ and are $\\mathcal A$-measurable. If a further independent fair matching switch in direction $e_i$ produces the energy $E_d$ by the same rule, then $$\\mathbb E[E_{d-1}-E_d\\mid\\mathcal A]\n \\ge\\frac{\\min(h_0,h_1)}N\\,\n             \\mathbb E[E_{d-1}\\mid\\mathcal A].\n \\label{shear:eq:delayed-symmetry-loss}$$ The statement includes $d=1$, when the intermediate schedule is empty.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-delayed-symmetry",
      "inputs": [
        "Earlier fixed available set and mass",
        "Independent intervening coordinate layers"
      ],
      "method": "Replace intermediate switches by law-preserving shear conjugates and average the final crossing.",
      "output": "Conditional half-space symmetry and delayed energy contraction.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-shear-lem-delayed-symmetry"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-fourier-inputs",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-fourier-inputs",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $u>0$, $C_u<\\infty$ and $Z_u(s)\\to0$ as $s\\to\\infty$.*\n\n*Let $H_1,\\ldots,H_r$ be subgroups of a finite group $G$, and let $\\mu$ be a probability measure. Write $\\mu_H$ for its image on $G/H$. If $$\\delta=\\sum_{i=1}^r\n \\|\\mu_{H_i}-U_{G/H_i}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}},$$ there is a measure $0\\le f\\le\\mu$, of mass $z\\ge1-\\delta$, with $$f(gH_i)\\le [G:H_i]^{-1}\\qquad(g\\in G,\\ 1\\le i\\le r).$$ It can be obtained by successive trimming of excess coset mass or by taking the pointwise minimum of the separately trimmed measures. When $z>0$, its normalization is within $1-z$ of $\\mu$ in TV and has coset cap $z^{-1}$ times uniform.*\n\n*Alternatively, delete the union of all cosets whose original $\\mu$-mass exceeds twice uniform. The deleted mass is $\\eta\\le2\\delta$. If $\\delta<1/2$, the normalized retained law is exactly $\\eta$ away from $\\mu$ in TV and has coset cap $2/(1-\\eta)$ times uniform.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-companion-inputs",
      "inputs": [
        "Companion degree and coset results"
      ],
      "method": "Import all-positive-power degree summability and common trimming results.",
      "output": "Retained-law caps and normalized comparison costs.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-shear-prop-fourier-inputs"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-disjoint-transfer",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-companion-inputs",
        "step-single-vector-orbits"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shear-prop-disjoint-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ have $N$ elements and $G=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(X)$. Let $M_1,\\ldots,M_r$ be disjoint nonempty subsets of $X$, where $r\\ge1$, and put $H_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(M_i)$, fixing the complement pointwise. Their union need not be $X$. Suppose $f\\ge0$ has mass $z\\le1$ and $$f(gH_i)\\le K[G:H_i]^{-1}\\qquad(g\\in G,\\ 1\\le i\\le r)$$ for some $K\\ge0$. For an irreducible unitary representation $\\rho$ of degree $D$, set $$S_i(D)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\widehat H_i\\\\ \\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/r}}}\n       (\\dim\\tau)^2.$$ For every $u>0$, the separate orbit and isotypic estimates are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n &\\le \\left(\\frac{zK}{D}\\sum_iS_i(D)\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\sqrt{zKrC_u}\\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2r)},\n \\label{shear:eq:noncovering-orbit-general}\\\\\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n &\\le \\frac{zK}{D}\\sum_iS_i(D)\n \\le zKrC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:noncovering-isotypic-general}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, when $r>4$, the inverse-square orbit estimate gives $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le\\sqrt{rKC_2}\\,D^{-(1-4/r)/2}.\n \\label{shear:eq:orbit-bound}$$ A separate coefficient-projection argument gives $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le\\frac{K^2}{D}\\sum_{i=1}^r\n       \\sum_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\widehat H_i\\\\\n                            \\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/r}}}(\\dim\\tau)^2\n \\le K^2rC_{10}D^{-1+12/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-general}$$ Finally, for $\\rho=\\rho_\\lambda$ with $\\lambda\\vdash N$, put $$k_\\lambda=N-\\max(\\lambda_1,\\lambda'_1),\n \\qquad M(k)=\\sum_{j=0}^k p(j),$$ where $p(j)$ is the partition number and $p(0)=1$. The branching refinement is $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho_\\lambda)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le zKrM(k_\\lambda)D^{-1+2/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-branching}$$ All these statements allow unequal support sizes and unrestricted restriction multiplicities. In particular they include the probability-law cases $z=1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-disjoint-transfers",
      "inputs": [
        "Disjoint support subgroups",
        "Capped retained mass"

      "method": "Use a common small-type cover for orbit assignment, central projections, coefficient norms, and branching counts.",
      "output": "Separate operator and Hilbert–Schmidt estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-shear-prop-disjoint-transfer"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-subprobability-fourier",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-plancherel"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-subprobability-fourier",
          "exact_quote": "For a complex mass function $a$ on a finite group $G$, $$\\sum_\\rho D_\\rho\\|\\widehat a(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n =|G|\\sum_g|a(g)|^2.\n \\label{shear:eq:plancherel-mass}$$ If $\\xi\\ge0$ has mass $m\\le1$, its transform is not divided by $m$, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n |G|\\sum_g\\left|\\xi(g)-\\frac{m}{|G|}\\right|^2\n &=\\sum_{\\rho\\ne\\mathrm{triv}}D_\\rho\n                   \\|\\widehat\\xi(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2,\n \\label{eq:subprobability-centered}\\\\\n \\left(\\sum_g|\\xi(g)-U_G(g)|\\right)^2\n &\\le(1-m)^2+\\sum_{\\rho\\ne\\mathrm{triv}}D_\\rho\n                   \\|\\widehat\\xi(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2.\n \\label{eq:subprobability-full}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $\\xi\\le\\mu$ for a probability measure $\\mu$, then $\\sum_g|\\mu(g)-\\xi(g)|=1-m$. When $m>0$, $\\|\\xi/m-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\le1-m$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-mass-normalization",
      "inputs": [
        "Complex mass functions",
        "Dominated subprobabilities"
      ],
      "method": "Apply complex Plancherel after centering by the retained mass.",
      "output": "Missing-mass and normalization inequalities.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-subprobability-fourier"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-shear-cor-512",
      "depends_on": [

The average over uniform ordered lists of $q$ distinct input labels of the total-variation distance of their outputs from a uniform injection is $o(1)$.     {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-matching-invariance",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-frame-construction",
        "step-frame-endpoints"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-matching-invariance",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $T\\ge d$ and directions $v_1,\\ldots,v_T$ such that every consecutive $d$ form a basis. There are invertible linear maps $L_0,\\ldots,L_T$ with $L_0e_i=v_i$ for $1\\le i\\le d$ such that, if $g_t$ is the cyclic-layer process, then $L_tg_tL_0^{-1}$ is the independent switch process with directions $v_t$. This assertion is an equality in law conditional on the entire fixed sequence. If $L_0=I$, the matching on trajectory labels before each switch is unchanged. For general $L_0$, the same statement holds after relabeling the initial labels by $L_0$. Consequently the average, over uniform ordered input lists of any fixed length, of the endpoint distance to a uniform injection is unchanged.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-matching-invariance",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed-schedule frames"
      ],
      "method": "Use selected-column identities to preserve matching trajectory labels.",
      "output": "Matching-functional invariance and input-average preservation.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-matching-invariance"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-holes",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-online-energy"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-holes",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $T\\ge1$ and nonzero directions $v_1,\\ldots,v_T$. Let $Z_0=I$ and $Z_t=\\xi_tZ_{t-1}$, where the $\\xi_t$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_t$. Fix complete feasible trajectories of some observed labels. If $m\\ge1$ labels remain unobserved, let $F_t$ be the positions not occupied by the observed labels at time $t$. For a specified unobserved label, let $p_t$ be its conditional position law given those complete observed trajectories.*\n\n*The vector $p_t$ evolves from its initial point mass by averaging on pairs with two positions in $F_{t-1}$ and deterministic transport on pairs with one such position. It depends only on the directions and observed paths through time $t$. The uniform vector $1/m$ is transported to the uniform vector on $F_t$. Thus, with $E_t=\\sum_{x\\in F_t}(p_t(x)-1/m)^2$, one has $0\\le E_t\\le1$ and $$E_{t-1}-E_t=\\frac12\\sum_{\\{x,y\\}\\subset F_{t-1}\\text{ paired at }t}\n                    (p_{t-1}(x)-p_{t-1}(y))^2.\n \\label{frames:hole-loss}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-complete-path-flow",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed feasible complete observed trajectories"
      ],
      "method": "Identify the observed-path event as a cylinder in the independent coin array.",
      "output": "Prefix-determined hidden mass despite full-path conditioning.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-holes"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-conditional-interface",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-complete-path-flow"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-conditional-interface",
          "exact_quote": "Fix integers $T\\ge1$ and $1\\le q\\le n$. Let $\\mathbf v=(v_1,\\ldots,v_T)$ be a random schedule of nonzero directions. Set $Z_0=I$, and suppose that, conditional on $\\mathbf v$, the increments of $Z$ are independent uniform switches in directions $v_1,\\ldots,v_T$. Let $C=(c_1,\\ldots,c_q)$ be a deterministic list of distinct labels, or a random such list independent of the joint process $(\\mathbf v,(Z_t)_{t=0}^T)$. For $1\\le j\\le q$, put $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal F_t^{C,j}\n &=\\sigma\\bigl(C,v_1,\\ldots,v_t,\\,\n       Z_s(c_i):0\\le s\\le t,\\ i<j\\bigr), \\label{frames:online-field}\\\\\n \\mathcal H^{C,j}\n &=\\sigma\\bigl(C,\\mathbf v,\\,\n       Z_s(c_i):0\\le s\\le T,\\ i<j\\bigr). \\label{frames:terminal-field}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $0\\le t\\le T$, define $p_t(x)=\\Pr\\{Z_t(c_j)=x\\mid\\mathcal F_t^{C,j}\\}$. Then, for every $x\\in V$, $$\\Pr\\{Z_t(c_j)=x\\mid\\mathcal H^{C,j}\\}=p_t(x)\n \\quad\\text{almost surely}. \\label{frames:full-path-prefix}$$ For $t<T$, let $K_t(v)=\\Pr\\{v_{t+1}=v\\mid v_1,\\ldots,v_t\\}$. Then $$\\Pr\\{Z_t(c_j)=x,\\ v_{t+1}=v\\mid\\mathcal F_t^{C,j}\\}\n =p_t(x)K_t(v). \\label{frames:direction-factorization}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-conditioning-fields",
      "inputs": [
        "Product-switch law conditional on directions",
        "Independent input list"
      ],
      "method": "Apply the tower property to complete and chronological fields.",
      "output": "Hidden-position and next-direction factorization.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-conditional-interface"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-cross-energy",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-online-energy"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-cross-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose, given the generated past, the next direction is uniform outside a hyperplane $J\\subset V$. Split the $m$ unobserved positions into the two cosets, denoting the resulting sets by $F_0,F_1$ and their sizes by $m_0,m_1$. For any centered real mass vector $z$ known from that past, the expected squared-norm loss obtained by averaging on the paired unobserved positions is $$\\frac1n\\sum_{x\\in F_0,\\ y\\in F_1}(z_x-z_y)^2\n \\ge \\frac{\\min(m_0,m_1)}n\\sum_xz_x^2.$$ If the next direction is uniform outside a smaller subspace contained in $J$, the same lower bound holds with $1/(2n)$ in place of $1/n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-centered-contraction",
      "inputs": [
        "Centered real vector",
        "Fresh direction"
      ],
      "method": "Apply the bipartite centered-vector identity to the matching loss.",
      "output": "Cross-hyperplane squared-norm contraction.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-cross-energy"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-crossing-balance",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-fixed-balance",
        "step-conditioning-fields",
        "step-centered-contraction"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-crossing-balance",
          "exact_quote": "Fix integers $T\\ge d$ and $r\\ge1$. For any fixed observed labels and any $d<t\\le T$, if $m$ positions remain unobserved, then $$\\Pr\\{\\min(|F_{t-1}\\cap J_t|,|F_{t-1}\\setminus J_t|)<m/4\\}\n \\le2e^{-m/8}.$$ For the hidden-card energy of Lemma <a href=\"#frames:holes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:holes\">38</a>, interpreted in this random process by Corollary <a href=\"#frames:conditional-interface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:conditional-interface\">39</a>, if $m\\ge n/r$ and $\\alpha=1/(4r)$, then $$\\mathbb E E_T\\le(1-\\alpha)^{T-d}+2e^{-n/(8r)}.\n \\label{frames:crossing-recursion}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-crossing-recurrence",
      "inputs": [
        "Observed labels",
        "Lower available-set size"
      ],
      "method": "Use fixed-schedule transverse balance and then average only in the chronological field.",
      "output": "Expected terminal energy bound.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-crossing-balance"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-backward-removal",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-conditioning-fields",
        "step-frame-construction"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-backward-removal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T\\ge d$ be an integer, and let $r\\ge2$ divide $n$. Partition $V$ into equal input blocks $M_1,\\ldots,M_r$ and, for each $i$, fix an order $c_{i,1},\\ldots,c_{i,q}$ on its complement, where $q=n-n/r$. Write $\\mathcal P$ for the partition together with these orders, and let $K_i=\\operatorname{Sym}(M_i)$ fix the complement pointwise. Use the variable-direction process just defined, whose first frame is $L_0=I$. For $1\\le j\\le q$, let $$\\mathcal H_{i,j}\n =\\sigma\\bigl(\\mathbf v,\\,\n       Z_s(c_{i,h}):0\\le s\\le T,\\ h<j\\bigr),\\qquad\n F_{i,j}=V\\setminus\\{Z_T(c_{i,h}):h<j\\},$$ and define $$p_{i,j}(x)=\\Pr\\{Z_T(c_{i,j})=x\\mid\\mathcal H_{i,j}\\},\\qquad\n E_{i,j}=\\sum_{x\\in F_{i,j}}\n       \\left(p_{i,j}(x)-\\frac1{n-j+1}\\right)^2.$$ Suppose $\\mathbb E E_{i,j}\\le\\varepsilon_n$ uniformly. Put $\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P}=\\bigcap_{i,j}\\{E_{i,j}\\le n^{-6}\\}$, and let $\\mu$ be the original cyclic endpoint law at time $T$. Then $\\Pr(\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P}^c)\\le rq n^6\\varepsilon_n$. The measure $$\\nu(g)=\\Pr\\{\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P},\\ L_T^{-1}Z_T=g\\}$$ satisfies $0\\le\\nu\\le\\mu$ and, for large $n$, $\\nu(gK_i)\\le2U_G(gK_i)$ for every $g\\in G$ and $1\\le i\\le r$. If $a=\\Pr(\\mathcal G_{\\mathcal P})=1-o(1)$, its normalized law $\\bar\\nu=\\nu/a$ satisfies $\\bar\\nu(gK_i)\\le4U_G(gK_i)$ and $\\|\\bar\\nu-\\mu\\|_{\\rm TV}\\le1-a=o(1)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-descending-removal",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed partition and complementary orders",
        "Uniform expected energy bound"
      ],
      "method": "Bound specified endpoints while discarding their energy constraints in descending order.",
      "output": "One retained measure with simultaneous pointwise coset caps.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-backward-removal"
    },

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-frames-stopped-marginals",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge4$. Put $b=16$, $m=n/b$, $q=n-m$, and $T=(2+20b)d=322d$. For every fixed ordered list of $q$ distinct input positions, its image under the physical shuffle at time $T$ has distance at most $$\\delta_d=\\frac{4T}{m}+\\frac12n^{3/2}\n                (1-1/(4b))^{(T-2d+1)/2}=o(1)\n \\label{frames:stopped-delta}$$ from a uniform injection, uniformly over the chosen list.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-reference-rows",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-complete-path-flow"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-reference-rows",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a realized cyclic history $g=(g_0,\\ldots,g_T)$ and a nonempty set $F$ of labels. For $u\\in F$, let $p_t^{u,F}(x)$ be the conditional probability under $\\mathbb P$ that card $u$ is at $x$ at time $t$, given the complete trajectories of the labels in $V\\setminus F$, evaluated on the trajectories supplied by $g$. Define a row indexed by $F$ by $$r_t^{u,F}(v)=p_t^{u,F}(g_t(v)),\\qquad v\\in F.$$ Then, for this realized history, $$r_0^{u,F}=e_u^{\\mathsf T},\\qquad\n r_t^{u,F}=r_{t-1}^{u,F}B_t^F,\n \\qquad r_t^{u,F}=e_u^{\\mathsf T}Q_t^F.\n \\label{frames:reference-row-flow}$$ Here $e_u$ is the coordinate vector in $\\mathbb R^F$. In particular the conditional likelihood of the realized endpoint of card $u$ is $$p_T^{u,F}(g_T(u))=Q_T^F(u,u).\n \\label{frames:diagonal-shadow}$$ The conditioning in this statement remains on the paths of $V\\setminus F$; the realized history enters $r_t^{u,F}$ through the indexing bijection $v\\mapsto g_t(v)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-reference-rows",
      "inputs": [
        "Realized cyclic history",
        "Observed complement"
      ],
      "method": "Reindex averaging and transport by realized trajectory labels.",
      "output": "Conditional row products and realized endpoint likelihoods.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-reference-rows"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-typical-history",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-matching-invariance",
        "step-reference-rows",
        "step-centered-contraction"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-typical-history",
          "exact_quote": "Define the event on cyclic histories by $$\\label{frames:typical-average}\n \\mathcal G_{\\mathrm{ent}}\n =\\bigcap_{m=n/L}^{n}\n   \\left\\{g:\\mathbb E_{F:\\,|F|=m}Z_T^F(g)\\le n^{-8}\\right\\},$$ where each average is uniform over subsets of labels. Then $\\mathbb P(\\mathcal G_{\\mathrm{ent}})=1-o(1)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-shadow-entropy",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-reference-rows",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-shadow-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "For every probability law $\\nu$ on histories supported on $\\mathcal G_{\\mathrm{ent}}$, $$\\label{frames:entropy-comparison}\n D(\\nu\\|\\mathbb P)\\ge\n       \\mathbb E_{X:\\,|X|=q}D(\\nu_X\\|U_X)-n^{-2}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-shadow-entropy",
      "inputs": [
        "Reference row likelihoods",
        "Typical history energy caps"
      ],
      "method": "Apply entropy chain rule and bound realized conditional likelihood excess on the common event.",
      "output": "Entropy comparison for every supported tilted law.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-shadow-entropy"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-entropy-tail",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-shadow-entropy",
        "step-typical-history"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-entropy-tail",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $C$ such that for every irreducible unitary representation $\\rho$ of $G$, of degree $D$, and unit vectors $u,z$, $$\\mathbb P\\{\\mathcal G_{\\mathrm{ent}},\n       |\\langle z,\\rho(g_T)u\\rangle|\\ge D^{-1/8}\\}\n \\le C D^{-1/(2L)}.\n \\label{frames:coefficient-tail}$$ Put $a=\\mathbb P(\\mathcal G_{\\mathrm{ent}})$. When $a>0$, the conditional endpoint law $\\eta=\\mathcal L(g_T\\mid\\mathcal G_{\\mathrm{ent}})$ is defined and satisfies $$\\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)\\|_{\\rm op}\n \\le D^{-1/8}+\\frac{C}{a}D^{-1/(2L)}.$$ For all sufficiently large $d$, Lemma <a href=\"#frames:typical-history\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:typical-history\">47</a> gives $a\\ge1/2$. In that range the last bound is at most $C'D^{-1/(2L)}$ for the absolute constant $C'=1+2C$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-entropy-tail",
      "inputs": [

      ],

      "output": "Coefficient tail and conditional endpoint operator bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-frames-entropy-tail"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-frames-entropy-application",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-entropy-tail",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-entropy-application",
          "exact_quote": "With $L=128$, $T=(1+100L)d$, and $b=32L$, the shuffle has full-permutation distance $o(1)$ at time $bT$.",

      "id": "step-entropy-amplification",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-fresh-hyperplane-energy**: For a centered real vector on the unobserved positions, averaging the matching loss over a fresh direction uniform outside the specified hyperplane equals the cross-pair expression and satisfies the smaller-half energy lower bound.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-noncentered-vector**: Apply the smaller-half lower bound to an arbitrary vector without checking centering. -> The cross-pair calculation alone does not establish the stated energy lower bound for a vector with nonzero total mass.
- **anti-use-biased-direction**: Use the same expected-loss expression for a biased or predetermined next direction. -> Cross pairs no longer have the matching probabilities used in the expectation.
- **anti-use-finite-check-as-mixing-proof**: Treat successful finite contraction checks as a certificate of full-permutation mixing. -> A local energy identity does not supply the marginal comparison, Fourier amplification, or asymptotic limit.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The fresh-direction contraction lemma gives a finite exact calculation for a centered vector on a specified available set. Enumerating the permitted directions and their unordered matching edges computes its expected loss; an independent cross-pair calculation checks the identity and lower bound. This certifies only the tested finite instances of the local lemma.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with d, mask, and z; null entries are unavailable positions.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= d <= 6; mask is a nonzero d-bit integer; available rational entries sum to zero.
# OUTPUTS: Exact rational energy, expected matching loss, lower bound, and comparison result as JSON.
# TIME: O(n*n) rational operations, where n = 2**d.
# SPACE: O(n) rational entries and direction losses.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic using Fraction.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

obj = json.load(sys.stdin)
d = obj['d']
assert type(d) is int and 1 <= d <= 6
n = 1 << d
mask = obj['mask']
assert type(mask) is int and 0 < mask < n
raw = obj['z']
assert isinstance(raw, list) and len(raw) == n
assert all(v is None or type(v) in (str, int) for v in raw)
z = [None if v is None else Fraction(v) for v in raw]
available = [x for x in range(n) if z[x] is not None]
assert available and sum((z[x] for x in available), Fraction(0)) == 0

def side(x):
    return (x & mask).bit_count() % 2

directions = [v for v in range(1, n) if side(v) == 1]
losses = []
for v in directions:
    loss = Fraction(0)
    for x in available:
        y = x ^ v
        if x < y and z[y] is not None:
            loss += (z[x] - z[y]) ** 2 / 2
    losses.append(loss)
loss = sum(losses, Fraction(0)) / len(directions)
energy = sum((z[x] ** 2 for x in available), Fraction(0))
counts = [sum(side(x) == b for x in available) for b in (0, 1)]
bound = Fraction(min(counts), n) * energy
result = {'energy': str(energy), 'loss': str(loss), 'lower_bound': str(bound), 'holds': loss >= bound}
print(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True))
```

2 * left * right\n    loss = cross_sum / n\n    lower_bound = energy * min(len(groups[0]), len(groups[1])) / n\n    gold = {'energy': str(energy), 'loss': str(loss), 'lower_bound': str(lower_bound), 'holds': loss >= lower_bound}\n    if type(candidate) is not dict or type(expected) is not dict:\n        raise ValueError('invalid output object')\n    if set(candidate) != set(gold) or set(expected) != set(gold):\n        raise ValueError('invalid output keys')\n    for output in (candidate, expected):\n        if type(output['holds']) is not bool:\n            raise ValueError('invalid comparison type')\n        for key in ('energy', 'loss', 'lower_bound'):\n            if type(output[key]) is not str:\n                raise ValueError('invalid rational output type')\n    if not gold['holds'] or candidate != gold or expected != gold:\n        raise ValueError('certificate mismatch')\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(1)\nsys.exit(0)\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The physical shuffle gives a concrete permutation sampler for an array of labeled elements whose length is a power of two. Its convergence guarantee is asymptotic and uniform over deterministic initial orderings. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A card-game simulation can implement the specified paired interleaving operation and reproduce the Thorp shuffle law on a dyadic deck. Individual-card uniformity does not certify a uniformly shuffled whole deck. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The conditional-mass recursion supplies a finite analysis routine for a hidden card when the direction schedule and feasible trajectories of observed labels are specified. It computes the posterior by averaging and transport rather than enumerating every hidden permutation. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Successive physical Thorp shuffles form an explicit randomized algorithm for approaching the uniform permutation law. The paper proves convergence in a horizon proportional to the coordinate dimension, while its support bounds obstruct prematurely short runs. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Permutations, available-position sets, and probability vectors are mathematical state representations here. The paper does not construct a data structure with an interface or operation guarantees. |
| hardware_accelerators | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The matching-switch model gives a concrete switching-network construction: each layer independently swaps or fixes disjoint pairs in a coordinate direction. This supports an architectural adaptation, without a hardware latency or resource guarantee. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper relates partial-card laws to enciphering small domains, but its shuffle model uses independent fair randomness. It supplies no keyed construction or computational security reduction that would justify treating a pseudorandom implementation as the analyzed law. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The entropy comparison concerns probability laws on shuffle histories and their endpoint restrictions. It does not define a learning model, training procedure, or prediction guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Selecting a partition that attains an average marginal-error bound is an existence argument in the mixing proof. The paper does not provide an optimization objective and solution procedure for routing, assignment, or scheduling. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The chronological fields describe what is revealed in a centralized stochastic permutation process. They do not specify communication, asynchronous execution, failures, or a distributed protocol. |
| other (Finite probability-measure processing) | True | strong | implementable | inferred | Simultaneous trimming explicitly constructs a nearby probability law with bounded masses across several subgroup coset systems when the summed marginal discrepancy satisfies the stated condition. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-dyadic-state-space** (hypothesis): The physical mixing conclusions concern dyadic decks and do not establish the same guarantees for arbitrary deck sizes. Blocked use: ['Apply the stated mixing horizons to an arbitrary array length without a separate argument.']
- **limit-independent-fair-switches** (hypothesis): The shuffle law requires independent fair switch choices; correlated, biased, or keyed replacements require their own comparison argument. Blocked use: ['Transfer the statistical mixing guarantee unchanged to a different source of switch choices.']
- **limit-physical-time-accounting** (formal_scope): The stated horizons count physical shuffles. They do not count whole coordinate sweeps or elementary implementation operations. Blocked use: ['Report a physical-shuffle horizon as a number of sweeps.', 'Infer implementation runtime directly from the mixing horizon.']
- **limit-asymptotic-accuracy** (approximation): Convergence with increasing deck dimension does not by itself supply a specified error tolerance for a particular finite deck. Blocked use: ['Advertise exact uniform sampling at the stated horizon.', 'Certify a finite-deck accuracy target solely from the asymptotic convergence statement.']
- **limit-no-cutoff-or-optimal-prefactor** (formal_scope): The paper does not assert an optimal leading constant or a cutoff statement. Blocked use: ['Identify the strongest stated upper horizon with the exact mixing threshold.', 'Infer a cutoff location or window.']
- **limit-short-horizon-support** (edge_case): The support lower bound obstructs early full-permutation mixing even before any Fourier analysis is used. Blocked use: ['Claim full-deck mixing at a horizon contradicted by the support estimate.']
- **limit-single-card-versus-joint-law** (formal_scope): Uniform individual-card locations do not establish uniformity of the full permutation because card trajectories share switches. Blocked use: ['Validate full-deck uniformity using only individual-card position frequencies.']
- **limit-frame-schedule-conditioning** (hypothesis): Frame maps are constructed after the complete direction schedule is fixed and may depend on future directions. Their existence does not make them available in a chronological observation field. Blocked use: ['Adjoin future-dependent frame data before drawing the next fresh direction.', 'Treat the fixed-schedule path comparison as an online sampling rule.']
- **limit-initial-input-map** (hypothesis): A general initial frame relabels the input list. Same-list equality requires the stated initial-frame condition or the separate shear coupling; independent uniform input averaging gives a different valid comparison. Blocked use: ['Apply a general-frame identity to the same prescribed list while dropping its input relabeling.']
- **limit-terminal-posterior-identification** (hypothesis): The terminal tuple comparison requires identification of the hidden posterior after terminal data and predecessor paths are disclosed. Additional auxiliary disclosure needs its own path-law justification. Blocked use: ['Apply the tuple bound after arbitrary extra conditioning without proving posterior identification.']
- **limit-unconditional-balance** (formal_scope): The fixed-list balance probability averages observed paths and is not a concentration bound conditional on those paths. Blocked use: ['Insert the unconditional imbalance probability as a conditional bound in every observed-path fiber.']
- **limit-fixed-omitted-fraction** (hypothesis): The general fixed-list estimate assumes a fixed integer omitted-fraction parameter dividing the deck size and the stated list-length restriction. Blocked use: ['Use the estimate for the complete input list.', 'Allow the omitted-fraction parameter to vary with dimension without checking the resulting bound.']
- **limit-average-versus-mixture** (formal_scope): The sampled-input estimates average distances with each input list fixed. The distance of the mixed output law does not supply that stronger family of conditional-distance estimates. Blocked use: ['Replace an average of conditional distances by the distance of a mixture when selecting a partition or building quotient kernels.']
- **limit-partition-selection** (nonconstructive): The favorable deterministic partition is selected by an averaging argument. An efficient procedure for finding that partition is not supplied by this selection step. Blocked use: ['Treat partition existence as an efficient partition-search algorithm.']
- **limit-common-trimming-and-positive-mass** (hypothesis): Simultaneous caps require a common retained law. Conditioning or normalization requires positive retained mass and the stated control of summed marginal errors. Blocked use: ['Normalize an empty retained measure.', 'Repair each coset marginal separately and assume that the repairs define a common permutation law.']
- **limit-disjoint-support-transfer** (hypothesis): The disjoint-support Fourier transfer assumes disjoint nonempty supports, nonnegative retained mass, and simultaneous coset caps. The supports may leave labels unused, but the same proof does not cover arbitrary overlapping supports. Blocked use: ['Apply the direct-product small-type argument to overlapping subgroup supports.', 'Use a coset-cap transfer without verifying the cap for every required coset system.']
- **limit-fourier-normalization-and-parity** (formal_scope): Retained Fourier transforms are not divided by their mass, and the Hilbert–Schmidt norm uses ordinary trace. Missing mass and the sign representation require separate accounting in amplification. Blocked use: ['Normalize retained transforms silently.', 'Use a dimension-decay estimate alone to discard the sign contribution.', 'Replace ordinary trace by dimension-normalized trace without changing the inequalities.']
- **limit-companion-dependent-fast-transfer** (formal_scope): The fastest stated full-deck consequence invokes the companion isotypic estimate and inverse-degree limit. It should be distinguished from the locally completed standalone proof. Blocked use: ['Present the fastest consequence as proved solely by the standalone local degree argument.']
- **limit-centered-fresh-direction-energy** (hypothesis): The local cross-hyperplane energy bound requires a centered vector known from the generated past and the stated conditional uniform direction law. Blocked use: ['Apply the smaller-half energy bound to an uncentered vector.', 'Retain the same expected-loss formula for a biased or predetermined direction.']
- **limit-chosen-history-orders** (hypothesis): Descending removal uses a fixed partition and fixed complementary orders, together with uniform expected energy control. It does not produce simultaneous caps for arbitrary adaptively chosen orders. Blocked use: ['Choose complementary orders after observing the history and reuse the same retained-event guarantee.']
- **limit-stopped-prescribed-kernels** (hypothesis): The stopped coupling preserves the prescribed real endpoint kernel rather than replacing it by a posterior conditioned on earlier coupling success. Nested balance events are needed to charge imbalance only once. Blocked use: ['Condition the real endpoint law on previous coupling success and reuse the original energy estimate.', 'Drop nesting while keeping the same exceptional-event cost.']
- **limit-common-event-all-tilts** (hypothesis): The entropy comparison holds for every probability law supported on the same typical event. A statement only for the original shuffle law would not justify conditioning on a coefficient-selected rare event. Blocked use: ['Apply the coefficient-tail transfer using an entropy comparison proved only under the original history distribution.']
- **limit-retained-kernel-mass-and-sign** (hypothesis): Quotient density deletion produces substochastic kernels, and the retained-kernel application separately controls the full sign multiplicity space. Blocked use: ['Treat deleted kernels as doubly stochastic.', 'Replace control of the full sign multiplicity operator by cancellation of a single scalar sign mean.']
- **limit-repaired-marginals-can-depend** (formal_scope): Simultaneous half-deck repair gives uniform conditional bijection marginals while allowing dependence between the coordinates. Blocked use: ['Factor the repaired joint distribution into independent uniform half-deck bijections.']
- **limit-character-block-order-and-phase** (hypothesis): Placing the pass before or after the mixing segment leads to different positive forms and different marginal information. Physical comparison also requires restoring the terminal rotation when necessary. Blocked use: ['Exchange image caps with preimage caps without changing the conjugation argument.', 'Assume every character-amplification block ends with no coordinate rotation.']
- **limit-finite-oracle-coverage** (formal_scope): Finite exact checks of the local contraction identity do not certify the asymptotic full-permutation theorem or its additional conditioning and Fourier arguments. Blocked use: ['Promote a successful local contraction check into a certificate of the full mixing theorem.']

For an irreducible unitary representation $\\rho$ of degree $D$, set $$S_i(D)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\widehat H_i\\\\ \\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/r}}}\n       (\\dim\\tau)^2.$$ For every $u>0$, the separate orbit and isotypic estimates are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n &\\le \\left(\\frac{zK}{D}\\sum_iS_i(D)\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\sqrt{zKrC_u}\\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2r)},\n \\label{shear:eq:noncovering-orbit-general}\\\\\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n &\\le \\frac{zK}{D}\\sum_iS_i(D)\n \\le zKrC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:noncovering-isotypic-general}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, when $r>4$, the inverse-square orbit estimate gives $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le\\sqrt{rKC_2}\\,D^{-(1-4/r)/2}.\n \\label{shear:eq:orbit-bound}$$ A separate coefficient-projection argument gives $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le\\frac{K^2}{D}\\sum_{i=1}^r\n       \\sum_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\widehat H_i\\\\\n                            \\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/r}}}(\\dim\\tau)^2\n \\le K^2rC_{10}D^{-1+12/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-general}$$ Finally, for $\\rho=\\rho_\\lambda$ with $\\lambda\\vdash N$, put $$k_\\lambda=N-\\max(\\lambda_1,\\lambda'_1),\n \\qquad M(k)=\\sum_{j=0}^k p(j),$$ where $p(j)$ is the partition number and $p(0)=1$. The branching refinement is $$\\|\\widehat f(\\rho_\\lambda)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le zKrM(k_\\lambda)D^{-1+2/r}.\n \\label{shear:eq:hs-branching}$$ All these statements allow unequal support sizes and unrestricted restriction multiplicities. In particular they include the probability-law cases $z=1$.",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For the Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ cards, we prove that the worst-start total-variation distance after $`32800d`$ physical shuffles tends to zero as $`d\to\infty`$. Combining our fixed-list estimate with the companion Fourier transfer improves this bound to $`512d`$ shuffles. These results follow from bounds on partially observed permutation laws in random coordinate frames.
